| hc+norm / head | 4.8 / 2.3 | 4.7 / 2.3 | |

`gpu expert-calls 30.0/token` → eg ≈ 1.9 ms per expert call against ~13 µs
of VRAM traffic per expert: the GPU expert group is not weight-bound and the
S-tile could not help it. `VK_PROF=1` split (host memcpy / descriptors /
record vs submit vs fence wait) taken next to locate it.

### P4b — tile shaders with shared-memory staging: measured and declined (2026-09-06 21:37–22:06)

`7d07c1a`: the R=8 tile's activations staged in the LDS in 32 KB slices
(the design the P4 spec had deferred), gated against the P4 binary with the
v1 `.spv` files on the pristine side (`PRISTINE_SHADERS`, added for this).
Oracle identical (bit-identical, as the lane/word argument predicts); profile
at the serving knob 125.9 → 143.4 ms/token; serve path **0.94× / 0.91× /
0.92×** at 21 / 384 / 1 230 tokens, decode unchanged → `speedup gate: FAIL`,
exit 3, the chain restored the v1 shaders. Why: each workgroup stages R × I
floats (128 KB) for its 8 output rows (16 KB of weights) — more traffic than
it saves — and lanes read the LDS at a stride of 8 floats (bank conflicts).
`VK_PROF` on P4 (v1) had already located the cost in the GPU (host phases
≤ 1.2 ms per expert-group submit; 85–183 ms of GPU time per layer-chunk; 0.8
ms of fence wait per tiled dense call): v1 is bound by its 64 scalar x loads
per lane per word. P4c (`d38bfed`, vec4 x loads, no barriers) is the informed
retry; gate pending.

## P6 step 1 (2026-09-06 22:08–22:30) — why Open WebUI loses the prefix: the single KV slot

`ttft_serve.py --multiturn --side-request --system <3 000-char prefix>`
(`bd5a140`), served binary (P4), 384-token user text under a stable system
prompt. Turn 1 → an Open WebUI-style title request (33 tokens, a different
prompt) → turn 2 `[system, A, reply, B]` → regenerate. Engine's own
`REUSE` line:

| config | turn 1 (1 292 tok) | side (33 tok) | turn 2 (1 355 tok) | REUSE | regen |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `--kv-slots 1`, `COLI_KDA_GPU=2` (serving config) | 187.5 s | 3.6 s | **191.6 s** | **0** | 181.5 s |
| `--kv-slots 4` (gateway's `conversation_cache_slot` routing), `COLI_KDA_GPU=0` | 195.7 s | 4.0 s | **3.2 s** | **1 339 / 1 355** | 184.7 s |

The side request evicts the conversation's only slot; with slots the
gateway's hash of system + first user message keeps the conversation on its
own slot and the title request goes elsewhere. Multi-slot needs the CPU
recurrence today: `coli_vk_kda_init` re-seeds the ONE device state per layer
on every `session_open`, so two live slots on the GPU path would share (and
corrupt) it. Cost of `COLI_KDA_GPU=0`: ~10 % decode (G12), ~5 % prefill
(132 vs 125 ms/token at 781 tokens). **Decision: serve with `--kv-slots 4`
and `COLI_KDA_GPU=0`** (`~/start_glm53.sh`, 2026-09-06 late) — turn 2 of a
conversation from minutes to seconds is worth 10 % of decode on an
interactive box; per-slot device state (P6b) recovers it later. Still open:
whether Open WebUI's system prompt is byte-stable across turns
(`<memory_context>`, date) — the server log's REUSE lines under real use
answer that.

### P4c — vec4 x loads in the tile shaders: gate PASS at 1.03×, landed (2026-09-06 22:12–23:00)

`d38bfed`: v1's global reads as two 16-byte loads per row per word, host
gate `I % 8 == 0`. Oracle identical (bit-identical); profile at the serving
knob 125.5 → 126.4 ms/token (neutral); serve path 21 / 384 / 1 230 tokens:
1.00× / **1.03×** (46.3 → 44.9 s) / 1.03× (176.0 → 171.3 s), decode
unchanged, `tworeq` identical → `speedup gate: PASS`, in service
(`8524cb05…`). The x-load hypothesis is therefore mostly wrong: neither
staging (P4b, 0.91×) nor vectorised loads (P4c, 1.03×) moved the GPU expert
group, which stays at ~58 ms/token, ~1.9 ms per expert call. The remaining
suspects are per-dispatch fixed cost (3 dispatches × ~80 experts per
layer-chunk in one command buffer) and occupancy at 8 output rows per
workgroup; the next step is **GPU-side timestamps** (VK_QUERY_TYPE_TIMESTAMP
around each expert's dispatches), not another shader guess.

### The gateway caps glm53 at one KV slot (2026-09-06 23:0x)

`~/start_glm53.sh` with `--kv-slots 4` failed to start: `ValueError: glm53
engine supports at most 1 KV slot(s)` (`openai_server.py` `serve()`,
`family.limits.max_kv_slots` from `family_registry.py`). The engine itself
takes `KV_SLOTS` up to `GLM53_MAX_SLOTS=16` and ran four slots correctly
under the harness (which constructs `Engine` directly). Service restored at
one slot with `COLI_KDA_GPU=2` (the CPU recurrence only pays with slots).
P6 is therefore blocked on the gateway's family limit — raise it for glm53
together with P6b (per-slot device state), since the limit and the
single-device-state hazard are the same fact seen from two sides.

## P6 landed (2026-09-06 23:05–23:31) — 4 KV slots in service; turn 2 through the live gateway in 3.1 s

`cb9c11e`: `family_registry` glm53 `max_kv_slots` 1 → 16 (= `GLM53_MAX_SLOTS`;
the 1 carried no justification); `glm53.c` forces `COLI_KDA_GPU=0` when
`KV_SLOTS > 1` (one device KDA state per layer until P6b), loudly;
`tworeq.py TWOREQ_SLOTS=N`. Gates:

- `tworeq` at 4 slots, request i on slot i % 4: **IDENTICAL** at
  `COLI_KDA_GPU=0` and at `=2` (the guard printed `KV_SLOTS=4: forcing
  COLI_KDA_GPU=0` and the result was identical).
- The side-request case **through the live gateway** (`ttft_serve.py --url
  http://127.0.0.1:8081`, the user's HTTP path, `--kv-slots 4`,
  `COLI_KDA_GPU=0`, residency 99.18 %): turn 1 (1 292 tokens) 171.1 s → title
  request (33 tokens) 3.9 s → **turn 2 (1 355 tokens) 3.13 s, REUSE 1 339 /

  `REUSE 1 0 1292 / REUSE 2 0 33 / REUSE 3 1339 1355 / REUSE 4 0 1292`.

Two false starts on the way, both instrument-side and both now fixed in the
harness: `--min-resident 90` without `--warm` refused the first live run
(89.45 %), and the harness's default model id (`glm53`) did not match the
served `glm-5.3-flash` (404 on every request) — it now asks `/v1/models` and
prints HTTP status + body on failure. **Serving since 23:30: `--kv-slots 4`,
`COLI_KDA_GPU=0`, binary `4dd110f2…` (P2+P3+P4+P4c+P6).** Cost: ~10 % decode,
~5 % prefill (P6b recovers it); gain: every follow-up turn of a conversation
prefills only its new tokens.

item that removes it. Open WebUI is parked until then; `chat.py` and

## P7 step 0 (2026-09-07 06:11–06:15) — memory context on: stable with one memory, per-turn with four

The open question from §P6 step 1, measured through Open WebUI 0.11.0's own
backend (`POST /api/chat/completions`, `features.memory=true`, a JWT minted
inside the container from its `WEBUI_SECRET_KEY`; the preset's `memory`
capability switched on in `webui.db` and the model list refreshed through
`GET /api/models` — the server caches presets in `app.state.MODELS`, a
database edit alone does nothing — and restored to off afterwards). Gateway
`--kv-slots 8`, served binary 4dd110f2… (P6), `max_tokens 48`.

| run | memories | turn 1 | turn 2 | REUSE turn 2 | slots |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| A: the owner's account as it is | 1 (`context`, `setup/openwebui`, 345 chars) | 127 tok, ttft 18.7 s | 177 tok, **ttft 2.1 s** | **162 / 177** | 5 → 5 |
| B: three temporary `context` memories added through the API, deleted after | 4 | 176 tok, ttft 20.6 s | 195 tok, **ttft 21.6 s** | **0 / 195** | 3 → 6 |

The memory query endpoint, asked with each turn's query (Open WebUI joins
the last seven user messages), ranked the four memories `[travel, cooking,
work, setup]` on turn 1 and `[cooking, travel, work, setup]` on turn 2.
`add_memory_context` (`utils/memory.py:290`) appends that ranked block to
the end of the leading system message, so in run B the system message
changed between turns, the engine found no common prefix and the gateway's
slot hash (system + first user message) moved the conversation to another
slot. With one memory there is nothing to reorder and the prefix holds.

Also seen: the first attempt (06:11) ran with the capability edit not yet
visible to the server — turn 1 was 16 tokens, the bare question — and an
`import open_webui` in a side process inside the container tried to run the
database migrations (`Can't locate revision d4c1a8e37b62`: the database is
ahead of the running image). Mint tokens with PyJWT, never import the
backend out of process.

**Decision (P7 spec, `P7-PREFIX-CKPT-SPEC-2026-09.md`):** the checkpoint
alone would leave every turn after the first re-prefilling the history for
any account with more than one memory; P7 is the engine checkpoint plus a
gateway-side pin that keeps the `<memory_context>` block byte-identical for
the life of a conversation, behind `COLI_PREFIX_PIN`.

## P7 (2026-09-07 08:39–12:25) — prefix checkpoints + the gateway pin: a new conversation with the tool block starts in 5.3 s instead of 24 minutes

Spec `P7-PREFIX-CKPT-SPEC-2026-09.md`. Engine: `glm53.c` gains prefix
checkpoints, the DeepSeek-V4 `v4_ckpt_*` pattern (`deepseek_v4.c:12470-12760`)
over the span list the segment adapter already declares (`glm53_segment_spans`)
— per DSA layer the `len` rows of latent/ikeys/igates, per KDA layer the whole
recurrence and conv window. Gateway: the 8th SUBMIT field for glm53 and
`COLI_PREFIX_PIN`. Knobs `GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT` (1), `_SLOTS` (4), `_MIN` (128),
`_DISK` (1), `_END` (0), `GLM53_LOGIT_DUMP`, `COLI_PREFIX_PIN` (0),
`COLI_PREFIX_PIN_TAGS` (`memory_context`), `COLI_CKPT_DIR`.

Gate `p7_gate.sh p709071034`, pristine `~/bench/glm53.p7base` (78cd7c52…,
P2+P3+P4+P4c+P6), candidate 19e28e72…, all five steps rc=0.

**Step 1 — `prefill_gate.sh` with `GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT=0`, `MIN_SPEEDUP=0.97`.**
Off, the candidate IS the pristine engine: teacher forcing IDENTICAL at 782
positions, last-position logits `cosine=1.0000000 max_abs=0` (bit-identical,
not merely close), argmax 7664 both.

| size | pristine ttft (both runs) | candidate ttft (both runs) | speedup | decode p/c |
|---:|---|---|---:|---|
| 30 | 6.7 s / 2.4 s | 6.7 s / 2.5 s | 0.99× | 5.6 / 5.6 |
| 300 | 44.6 s / 43.9 s | 45.3 s / 44.4 s | 0.99× | 5.0 / 4.9 |
| 1000 | 168.4 s / 167.9 s | 172.0 s / 172.0 s | 0.98× | 3.9 / 3.9 |

**Step 2 — `ttft_serve.py --prefix-ckpt --oracle`**, `--tools
~/bench/owui_tools.json` (34 Open WebUI builtin tools) `--system
~/bench/p6_system.txt`, `--kv-slots 4`, residency ≥ 99.4 %. Three
conversations that share the prefix S and differ only in the user turn, so
slot reuse cannot fire for any of them:

| request | prompt tokens | reused | ttft |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| conv 1 `[S, A]` (cold; `CKPT plan prefix=6328 src=hint`, `CKPT store 370 MB`) | 6 342 | 0 | **1 452.16 s** |
| conv 2 `[S, B]`, another slot | 6 346 | **6 328** | **5.30 s** |
| conv 3 `[S, C]` after killing and respawning the engine (`CKPT disk load`) | 6 346 | **6 328** | **9.72 s** |
| conv 2 `[S, B]` again on an engine with `GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT=0` | 6 346 | 0 | 1 427.57 s |

t1/t2 = **274.18×**. The boundary came from the gateway hint, not the LCP
plan (`src=hint`): 6 328 tokens = `[gMASK]<sop>` + the tool block + the system
message, and the engine accepted it because re-tokenising those bytes
reproduced the prompt's first 6 328 ids exactly.

**The split prefill is not bit-identical, and the number is small.** Serving
`[S, B]` from a checkpoint (prefill split at 6 328, then a 18-token tail)
against serving it in one pass with checkpoints off: greedy text **IDENTICAL**
over 56 tokens / 299 bytes, first-token logits `cosine=1.0000000`,
**`max_abs=2.146e-05`**, argmax 29862 both. The 4-tool development run gave
`max_abs=2.098e-05` on a different prompt, so this is the scale of the effect,
not one lucky prompt. Cause: the two paths chunk the 128-token prefill loop
differently, so the KDA scan associates differently within a chunk. This is
the same class and the same magnitude as P4c's, and it is why the serve-path
logit dump (`GLM53_LOGIT_DUMP`) had to exist — the CLI teacher-forcing oracle
cannot see a split that only happens on the serve path.

**Step 3 — `tworeq.py TWOREQ_SLOTS=4`: IDENTICAL across 3 requests at
`COLI_KDA_GPU=0` and at `=2`.** (Note for whoever reads this next: with
identical prompts no checkpoint is captured or restored, so this proves the
new state home breaks nothing, not that a restore is slot-clean. The restore's
own oracle is step 2.)

**Step 4 — `--pin-block`** (engine mode, `COLI_PREFIX_PIN=1`): turn 2 carries
the same `<memory_context>` block with its three items re-ordered, which is
what Open WebUI produced with four memories.

| turn | prompt tokens | reused | ttft |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 `[S+X, A]` | 965 | 0 | 126.82 s |
| 2 `[S+Y, A, reply, B]` | 1 005 | **988** (17 prefilled) | **3.00 s** (0.024×) |

**Step 5 — checkpoints cost nothing when they cannot help.** The same 384-token
prompt three times, checkpoints ON: 49.64 / 44.48 / 44.56 s, `reused 0` every
time, no CKPT line at all (the LCP plan refuses a prefix with fewer than 8 tail
tokens, and the hint lands at `[gMASK]<sop>` = 2 tokens < MIN). Step 1's
candidate median 44.85 s vs step 5's 44.56 s = **1.007×**.

### In service, through the live gateway

`~/start_glm53.sh` gains `export GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT=1` and `export
COLI_PREFIX_PIN=1`; `--kv-slots 8` and `COLI_KDA_GPU=0` unchanged. Live check A
ran against a gateway restarted after the gate, i.e. with the gate's
checkpoints on disk and nothing in memory:

| request | prompt tokens | reused | ttft |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| `CKPT disk load prefix=6328 (370 MB)` + `prefix=911 (187 MB)` on the first SUBMIT | | | |
| conv 1 `[S, A]`, a conversation this process has never seen | 6 342 | **6 328** | **8.93 s** |
| conv 2 `[S, B]` | 6 346 | **6 328** | **5.46 s** |

That is the disk-persistence claim end to end: a checkpoint written by one
process is restored by another after a restart, and the first turn of a new
conversation with the full tool block costs seconds. Live check B (`p7b`,
12:22–12:25, same served binary):

| run | turn 1 | turn 2 | pin |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1, empty checkpoint dir | 965 tok, reused 0, **125.01 s** | 1 005 tok, reused **988**, **3.08 s** (0.025×) | `[pin] key=86ccf572 new block=170` |
| 2, immediately after | 965 tok, reused **911**, **8.39 s** | 1 005 tok, reused **988**, **3.08 s** | `[pin] … hit block=170` |

**Serving since 2026-09-07 12:25, binary 19e28e72…** (P2+P3+P4+P4c+P6+P7).

### What the spec got wrong, and one criterion that had to change

- **`--pin-block`'s ttft bound is only meaningful when turn 1 is cold.** The
  spec asks for `ttft(turn 2) < 0.25 × ttft(turn 1)`. Through the live gateway
  turn 1 is *itself* restored from the checkpoint on disk (8.39 s instead of
  125 s), so the ratio compares warm with warm and cannot reach 0.25 however
  well the pin works — it read 0.367× while the reuse count hit its bound
  exactly (988 ≥ 988). The harness now applies the ratio only when turn 1
  reused nothing and lets the reuse count decide otherwise; `--prefix-ckpt`
  carries the same branch, and both print which one fired. This is a
  miscalibrated instrument, not a slower engine: run 1 above, with a cold turn
  1, is 0.025×.
- **`GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT_END` (kind-1, prompt-end captures) is implemented and
  left OFF.** At 6 328 tokens a capture is 370 MB and ~0.3 s of memcpy per
  request; nothing in P7's cases needs it, and DSV4's reason for it (a client
  that re-renders the assistant reply) is P6's slot reuse here.
- The tool fixture is 34 tools, not the 24 the spec quotes, so the prefix is
  6 328 tokens rather than ~5 400 and the cold turn is 1 452 s rather than 876.
  Same phenomenon, bigger prompt.
- Two harness traps, both now fixed in the tree because both produced a wrong
  verdict once: the pin must run **before** the slot hash, not inside
  `render()` (pinning after `conversation_cache_slot` sent turn 2 to another
  slot: reused 0/115); and a step must **wait for its engine to die** before
  the next one starts, because `tworeq.py` returned while its engine still
  held the binary open — which made the next two steps refuse *and* made the
  failing chain's `cp` of the pristine binary fail with ETXTBSY, leaving the
  candidate in service after a failed gate. `~/bench/p7_stop.sh` and
  `wait_no_engine()` in `p7_gate.sh` are that fix.

The P7 step 0 run-B shape again — three temporary `context` memories added

on for the run and restored — against the served P7 gateway
(`COLI_PREFIX_PIN=1`, binary 19e28e72…):

| turn | prompt tok | REUSE | ttft | note |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| 1 `[A]` | 177 | 0 | 28.5 s | cold; `[pin] key=b16d9928 new block=631` |
| 2 `[A, reply, B]` | 205 | **190 / 205** | **2.2 s** (5.0 s end to end) | `[pin] … hit block=631`: the block Open WebUI re-ranked was replaced by turn 1's |
| 3 `[A, reply, B, "Spicy Thai food.", C]` | 223 | 0 | 26.5 s | the test sent a *fabricated* turn-2 reply instead of the real one, so the history diverged from what the engine generated — the regenerate case, correct by design, not a P7 miss |

Before P7 the same shape reused 0/195 on turn 2 (§P7 step 0). One
observation for the churn question in the spec: turn 3's fresh prefill
made the engine plan an LCP capture of the 177-token turn-1 prefix
(`CKPT plan prefix=177 src=lcp`, 162 MB stored and written to disk) — a
conversation-local prefix, not a shared one. It is bounded by the 4 LRU
slots and harmless, but `GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT_MIN=128` lets small
per-conversation prefixes through; raising it to ~512 (DSV4's default)
once the tool block is the only prefix worth keeping would stop those
writes. Not changed here.

### P7b (2026-09-09 07:42–08:49) — capture beyond a restored checkpoint: a new UI chat starts warm in 20.98 s instead of 362.94 s

`serve_one` planned a prefix capture only when `shared == 0`, and after a
checkpoint restore `shared` is not 0. A **partial** checkpoint was therefore
terminal: once one existed, the rest of the stable prefix was never captured
and every new conversation restored the partial prefix and re-prefilled the
same tail forever. Seen in service this morning on the CANCEL binary
(f41fc5cc…), `~/glm53_server.log`:

```
06:50:38 CKPT plan prefix=4011 src=hint      (an API-shaped prompt, captured whole)
07:04:27 CKPT plan prefix=2163 src=lcp       (a UI-shaped prompt: the LCP won over a longer hint)
07:17:24 REUSE 5 0 4649                      ttft 712.93 s
07:18:05 CKPT hit prefix=2163                (no CKPT plan line at all)
07:24:26 REUSE 6 2163 4424                   ttft 380.14 s
```

The change is the decision block in `serve_one` and nothing else
(`perf/p7b-capture-after-restore`, commit 10b1ef6):

- `ckpt_restore` sets `restored`, and plan/hint now run when `shared == 0` **or**
  when `shared` came from a restore — never when it came from slot reuse, whose
  session is continuing a conversation and must not be split for a copy;
- after a restore only a boundary strictly beyond `shared` is taken
  (`ckpt_at > shared` was already the split condition) and never one already
  stored;
- between plan and hint the **longer** wins. The hint is the boundary the
  gateway knows is stable (the pinned block start, else the first `<|user|>`);
  the plan is the LCP with the last fresh prompt, and on 2026-09-09 it was the
  shorter of the two — 2 163 instead of ~4 300 — which is the line above that
  did the damage;
- `ckpt_plan` still runs on every fresh prompt, so its `prev_ids` bookkeeping is
  unchanged, and the verbose line now says `src=lcp|hint` and `after-restore`.

Numerics: the two-call prefill split at `ckpt_at` already existed (P7); this
only decides where it lands. `GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT=0` is still the pristine path.

Gate `p7b_gate.sh p7b09090742`, chain `p7b_chain.sh`, pristine
`~/bench/glm53.cancel` (f41fc5cc…, the CANCEL binary in service), candidate
**f68d1cac…**, all five steps rc=0.

**Step 1 — `prefill_gate.sh` with `GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT=0`, `MIN_SPEEDUP=0.97`,
`PRISTINE_SHADERS=~/bench/shaders_cancel`.** Off, the candidate IS the pristine
engine: teacher forcing IDENTICAL at 782 positions, last-position logits
`cosine=1.0000000 max_abs=0` (bit-identical), argmax 7664 both.

| size | pristine ttft (both runs) | candidate ttft (both runs) | speedup | decode p/c |
|---:|---|---|---:|---|
| 30 | 2.89 s / 2.78 s | 2.90 s / 2.76 s | 1.00× | 5.8 / 5.8 |
| 300 | 40.94 s / 40.72 s | 40.68 s / 40.50 s | 1.01× | 6.0 / 6.0 |
| 1000 | 141.05 s / 140.56 s | 139.33 s / 138.87 s | 1.01× | 4.8 / 4.9 |

**Step 2 — `tworeq.py TWOREQ_SLOTS=4`: IDENTICAL across 4 requests at
`COLI_KDA_GPU=2` and at `=0`, zero `forcing COLI_KDA_GPU=0` lines** (the guard
must stay silent at knob 2, or the run measured the CPU recurrence).

**Step 3a — `ttft_serve.py --prefix-ckpt-partial --oracle`, THE P7b CASE**
(4-tool fixture `~/bench/owui_tools_4.json` + `~/bench/p6_system.txt`,
`--kv-slots 4`, private `COLI_CKPT_DIR`, residency 100.0000 %). Three
conversations sharing the tool block and a system message S; conv 2 and 3 carry
a **second** system message T:

| request | prompt tokens | reused | ttft | the engine's own lines |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| conv 1 `[S, A]` | 1 540 | 0 | **181.98 s** | `CKPT plan prefix=1527 src=hint`, `CKPT store 208 MB` |
| conv 2 `[S, T, B]` | 2 040 | **1 527** | **68.38 s** | `CKPT hit prefix=1527`, **`CKPT plan prefix=2023 src=hint after-restore`**, `CKPT store 224 MB` |
| conv 3 `[S, T, C]` | 2 040 | **2 023** | **2.92 s** | `CKPT hit prefix=2023` |

conv 1 → conv 3 is **62.3×**. On the pristine engine conv 2 prints no plan and
conv 3 reuses 1 527 of 2 040, i.e. it re-prefills the 496 tokens of T on every
conversation — that is the whole item, and it is why this step is a gate and
not a printout.

Oracle (conv 3 against the same binary with `GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT=0`): greedy text
**IDENTICAL** over 35 tokens / 178 bytes, `cosine=0.9999986`
**`max_abs=0.04251`**, argmax 154842 both.

**Two chunk boundaries in a state cost more than one, by about 2 000×.** Step
3b's oracle is the control: one split (P7's shape, the same fixture and the
same first token) gives `max_abs=2.301e-05`, the scale P7 recorded. Conv 3's
state above went through *two* extra boundaries — conv 1's capture at 1 527 and
conv 2's at 2 023 — so the KDA scan associates differently over 2 023 positions
instead of over one chunk. Same class, same knob (`GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT=0` is the
one-pass path), bigger number, and the gate criterion (cosine ≥ 1−1e-4, argmax
equal, text identical) is met with room.

**The second system message is the fixture, and the obvious one does not work.**
A "2 tools versus 4" pair cannot test this at all: glm53 renders the tool block
*before* the system message (`render_chat_glm53`: effort line, tool block,
system, user), so a 2-tool prefix has the tool epilogue where the 4-tool prompt
has tool 3, the bytes diverge inside the captured span, and `ckpt_restore` could
never fire. `<|system|>` is a special token, so a prefix that ends at the end of
S ends on a token boundary the longer prompt also has.

**Step 3b — `--prefix-ckpt --oracle`: P7's own case is untouched.**

| request | prompt tokens | reused | ttft |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| conv 1 `[S, A]` | 1 540 | 0 | 182.07 s |
| conv 2 `[S, B]`, another slot | 1 544 | **1 527** | **2.84 s** (t1/t2 = **64.13×**) |
| conv 3 `[S, C]` after killing and respawning the engine (`CKPT disk load`) | 1 544 | **1 527** | **2.43 s** |
| conv 2 `[S, B]` on an engine with `GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT=0` | 1 544 | 0 | 182.68 s |

Oracle: text IDENTICAL over 58 tokens / 294 bytes, `cosine=1.0000000
max_abs=2.301e-05`, argmax 154842 both.

**Step 3c — `--pin-block`:** turn 1 971 tokens, reused 0, 104.20 s; turn 2
1 010 tokens, reused **994** (16 prefilled, bound 994), **2.09 s** = 0.020×.

### P7b live, through Open WebUI's own backend

`~/bench/owui_ui_warm.sh` sends one UI-shaped turn from a throwaway saved chat
(saved chat + `session_id` + memory on — the exact shape the browser sends) and
deletes it. Two runs with **different** questions, so slot reuse cannot be what
makes the second one fast. Served binary f68d1cac…, `~/start_glm53.sh`
unchanged (`--kv-slots 4`, `COLI_KDA_GPU=2`, `GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT=1`,
`COLI_PREFIX_PIN=1`), the gateway's own `.coli_ckpt` (holding the 2 163-token
partial checkpoint) untouched by the gate:

| run | prompt tok | REUSE | ttft | the engine's own lines |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| 1 "Say OK." | 4 424 | 2 163 | 362.94 s | `CKPT disk load 4011 / 2163 / 910 / 381`, `CKPT hit prefix=2163`, **`CKPT plan prefix=4308 src=hint after-restore`**, `CKPT store prefix=4308 302 MB` |
| 2 "Reply with the single word ready." | 4 428 | **4 308** | **20.98 s** | `CKPT hit prefix=4308` |

Run 1 is the last slow turn of that shape: it pays the tail once and captures
it. Run 2 is what the owner needed — **a new chat in his browser starts warm**:
20.98 s against the 380.14 s the same shape cost at 07:24 this morning
(**18.1×**), and against 362.94 s in this pair (17.3×).

**The churn this makes visible, with its price.** A fresh prompt that restores a
checkpoint and then shares one more token with the *previous* fresh prompt now
captures again: 1 534 after 1 527 (208 MB), 2 028 after 2 023 (225 MB), 4 419
after 4 308 (305 MB) — conversation-local prefixes, exactly the churn the P7
section flagged, now reachable after every restore. The price per request is
~33 ms of device sync, ~110–160 ms of memcpy and one synchronous ~300 MB write
into the page cache, i.e. ~2 % of run 2's 20.98 s; it does not evict the useful
prefix, because the restored checkpoint is the most recently used one and the
victim rule takes the LRU. It is still 300 MB of disk write to gain five tokens.
The fix is a minimum *gain* rather than a minimum *length* (or
`GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT_MIN` raised to ~512, which the P7 section already proposed
for the same reason). **Not changed here** — the rule this item was given is
"strictly beyond `shared`", the gate measures that rule, and the cost is
recorded rather than tuned away.

## P6b (2026-09-07 15:39–16:56) — one KDA state set per KV slot: the CPU recurrence P6 forced is gone, +13.2 % decode at four slots

Spec `P6B-KDA-SLOT-STATE-SPEC-2026-09.md`, built on `perf/p6b-kda-slots`.
`G.kda[layer]` keeps the model weights (conv taps, `alog`, `dt`, `onorm`) once;
the recurrence state and the conv window become `G.kda_slot[layer][slot]`, and
`coli_vk_kda_pool_init` allocates every slot's set for every KDA layer in ONE
pass **before** `vk_preload_tier`. `coli_vk_kda_init/step/layer/sync/upload`
take the slot; `GSession` carries it; P7's `ckpt_sync_out/in` and the segment
adapter's `glm53_kda_sync_out/in` pass `s->slot`. `slots_init` no longer forces
`COLI_KDA_GPU=0` on `KV_SLOTS > 1` — it forces it, loudly, only when
`coli_vk_kda_pool_slots() < g_n_slots`.

Gate `p6b_gate.sh p6b09071539`, pristine `~/bench/glm53.p6bbase` (19e28e72…,
the P7 binary in service), candidate 53ccbb42…, all six steps rc=0. Chain
`~/bench/p6b_chain.sh`.

**Step 1 — `tworeq.py TWOREQ_SLOTS=4`, and this time the guard has to stay
silent.** Before P6b an IDENTICAL at four slots proved nothing about device
state, because `slots_init` forced the CPU recurrence and the run measured
that; the gate now counts the `forcing COLI_KDA_GPU=0` lines and requires 0 at
the GPU knob.

| knob | result | `forcing` lines | pool line |
|---|---|---:|---|
| `COLI_KDA_GPU=2` | **IDENTICAL** across 4 requests, one per slot | **0** | `[VK] KDA slot pool: 4 x 156 MB (34 KDA layers, 624 MB total)` |
| `COLI_KDA_GPU=0` | IDENTICAL across 4 requests | 0 | (no pool: the knob is off) |

156 MB per slot is the spec's arithmetic reproduced exactly: 34 × (64·128·128·4 B
state + 3·8192·4·4 B window).

**Step 2 — `prefill_gate.sh` at one slot, `MIN_SPEEDUP=0.97`, checkpoints off.**
With one slot the pool holds one set and the candidate must BE the pristine
engine. It is, bit-identically: teacher forcing IDENTICAL at 782 positions,
last-position logits `cosine=1.0000000 max_abs=0`, argmax 7664 both.

| size | pristine ttft (both runs) | candidate ttft (both runs) | speedup | decode p/c |
|---:|---|---|---:|---|
| 30 | 6.6 s / 2.4 s | 6.6 s / 2.3 s | 1.00× | 5.6 / 5.6 |
| 300 | 44.5 s / 43.8 s | 45.1 s / 44.6 s | 0.98× | 5.1 / 5.1 |
| 1000 | 168.1 s / 168.0 s | 171.8 s / 171.6 s | 0.98× | 3.8 / 3.9 |

**Step 3 — decode at four slots, the item's actual claim.** `ttft_serve.py
--sizes 30 --repeat 2 --gen 64`, one engine at a time, `COLI_KDA_GPU=2` on all
three rows (the pristine forces itself to 0 at four slots and prints so).

| row | decode tok/s, both runs | median |
|---|---|---:|
| candidate, 4 slots, GPU recurrence | 5.633 / 5.772 | **5.702** |
| pristine, 1 slot, GPU recurrence | 5.654 / 5.801 | 5.728 |
| pristine, 4 slots (guard forces the CPU recurrence) | 4.990 / 5.089 | 5.039 |

**0.996× against the 1-slot GPU number (bound 0.99) and 1.132× against the
4-slot CPU one (bound 1.08).** The +13.2 % is the ~10 % the roadmap projected
and sits beside G12's +11.7 % serving figure; the roadmap's "3.4 → 3.8 tok/s"
was written for a longer context, at this one it is 5.04 → 5.70.

**Step 4 — the live side-request case, through the serving gateway** (4 slots,
`COLI_KDA_GPU=2`, `--url http://127.0.0.1:8081`), i.e. the P6 case re-run on
the configuration P6 could not use:

| request | slot | prompt tokens | reused | ttft |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| turn 1 `[A]` | 0 | 1 292 | 0 | 170.25 s |
| side request (title) | 1 | 33 | 0 | 3.52 s |
| turn 2 `[A, reply, B]` | 0 | 1 355 | **1 339** | **3.08 s** (0.018×) |
| regenerate `[A]` | 0 | 1 292 | 910 (a P7 checkpoint) | 60.32 s |

`forcing COLI_KDA_GPU=0` lines in the server log: **0**. The engine's own
startup lines: `[VK] KDA slot pool: 4 x 156 MB` and `KV_SLOTS=4: KDA device
slot pool holds 4 -- COLI_KDA_GPU=2 stays on`.

**Step 5 — the device→host sync, which was the other half of the item.** P7's
capture ran at `COLI_KDA_GPU=0`, where no device state is read back; at the GPU
knob the old path was a memcpy from mapped write-combined memory, measured
~14 MB/s (p2c, 150 MB in 11 s), i.e. ~11 s per capture. It now records a
`vkCmdCopyBuffer` into HOST_CACHED staging.

| prefix | checkpoint | `sync` | `copy` (host blob) | KDA spans / sync |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 6 328 tokens (the 34-tool block) | 370 MB | **69 ms** | 202 ms | 156 MB → **2 262 MB/s** |
| 910 tokens, live gateway, slot 2 | 187 MB | 129 ms | 97 ms | 156 MB → 1 209 MB/s |

Bound was 1 000 ms. **161× off the write-combined path**, and the first time a
P7 checkpoint has been captured and restored at the GPU knob at all: conv 1
`[S, A]` 1 439 s cold, conv 2 `[S, B]` **5.15 s** (6 328 of 6 346 reused),
conv 3 after killing and respawning the engine 9.63 s — t1/t2 = **279.27×**,
within noise of P7's 274.18× at the CPU knob.

**Step 6 — what the pool cost.** The pool is allocated before the preload, so
it is paid in experts and not out of dev0's 3.0 GB reserve:

| engine | `[VK] preload: N heat-ranked experts` | KDA slot pool |
|---|---:|---|
| pristine, 1 slot | 1 296 | — |
| candidate, 4 slots | **1 248** | 4 × 156 MB |

**48 experts, 3.7 % of dev0's tier** (the spec projected 44; the preload's
budget check runs every 8, which is where the extra 4 come from). Expert-cache
hit rate from the `DONE … STAT` line: 100.00 % on both — the bound (0.5 points)
is met, but at these prompt sizes the metric is saturated and therefore does
not discriminate; the preload count and step 3's decode are what carry the
claim.

**In service since 2026-09-07 16:50, binary 53ccbb42…** (P2+P3+P4+P4c+P6+P7+P6b).
`~/start_glm53.sh`: `--kv-slots 8 → 4` and `COLI_KDA_GPU=0 → 2`, with
`GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT=1 COLI_PREFIX_PIN=1` unchanged. Four slots and not eight
because four is the pool the gate measured and eight would cost 88 experts for
slots Open WebUI does not use.

### What the spec got wrong, and two traps in the measurement

- **`coli_vk_kda_pool_init(nslots, heads, k, v, kernel)` cannot know how much
  to allocate.** The per-layer arrays are sized `VK_KDA_LAYERS = 64` for
  headroom and GLM-5.3 has 34 KDA layers; the spec's signature would have
  allocated the headroom — 1 173 MB at four slots instead of 624, ~83 experts
  instead of 48. The built signature takes the layer count and a `kda_layer[]`
  predicate, which `model_load` fills from `c->is_full[]`.
- **The spec's step 5 reads a `CKPT store … in <ms> ms` line that does not
  exist.** P7's line carries prefix / MB / kind / slot and no timing at all, so
  the criterion as written was not measurable. `ckpt_sync_out` now times itself
  and `ckpt_store` prints `sync=<ms> ms copy=<ms> ms`; the gate reads `sync=`.
  Same shape of correction as G12's "cosine at decode token 64", which the CLI
  also could not report.
- **The gate's first draft would have measured P7, not P6b.** `prefill_gate.sh`
  runs the pristine side first and the candidate second against ONE checkpoint
  directory, and its 30/300/1000 prompts share a prefix: with checkpoints on
  the 1 000-token run plans an LCP capture at ~300 tokens and persists it, so
  the candidate's first 1 000-token run would restore what the pristine's runs
  stored and look faster for having gone second. Caught before the numbers were
  taken; steps 2 and 3 now run with `GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT=0`, and the gate keeps
  its own `COLI_CKPT_DIR` so it never touches the gateway's store either.
- **`ttft_serve --multiturn` prints its ratio but appends no verdict**, so the
  process exits 0 even when turn 2 re-prefilled the whole history. Step 4 reads
  the recorded REUSE counts instead: ≥ 80 % of turn 2's prompt reused and
  ttft(2)/ttft(1) ≤ 0.35.
- The write-combined read path is worth restating as a number, because it is
  the one that decided the design: 14 MB/s vs 2 262 MB/s is the difference
  between a checkpoint capture at the serving knob being impossible and being
  free.

## RP4 (2026-09-07 17:00–20:07) — GPU timestamps on the expert group: the 58 ms/token bucket is 92 % CPU experts, and the GPU makes the engine wait 0.07 ms/token

RP4 is a profiling item; its deliverable is the measurement and its reading.
It exists because §P4c closed with "the GPU expert group stays at ~58 ms/token,
~1.9 ms per expert call; the remaining suspects are per-dispatch fixed cost
and occupancy at 8 output rows per workgroup". **Both suspects are wrong, and
so is the premise: there is no 58 ms/token of GPU work to attack.**

### The instrument (`COLI_VK_TIMESTAMPS`, off by default)

`c/backend_vulkan.c`: one `VK_QUERY_TYPE_TIMESTAMP` pool per device
(`vkts_setup`, called from each device's init), reset and written inside the
expert group's own command buffer on all three devices —

- `=1` — three queries: `TOP_OF_PIPE` at the top, `BOTTOM_OF_PIPE` after the
  fused gate+up phase (where the existing pipeline barrier already drains the
  queue, so the query costs nothing structurally) and after the down phase.
- `=2` — the above plus one `BOTTOM_OF_PIPE` query after **each expert's**
  dispatch in each phase (3 + 2·count ≤ 131 queries; the pool holds 160). This
  one is not structurally free: a `BOTTOM_OF_PIPE` timestamp drains the queue
  on RADV, so a phase's experts stop overlapping. Its phase totals read next to
  `=1`'s say how much overlap `=1` had — **and they are the same to within
  1 %, so the ~80 dispatches in a command buffer were already serial.**
- Read back with `vkGetQueryPoolResults` after the fence, converted with
  `timestampPeriod` (10.0 ns on RADV NAVI31, 64 valid bits).
- `VK_EXT_calibrated_timestamps` is asked for **only when the knob is set**, so
  a run with it off creates exactly the device it created before. It maps the
  device clock onto `CLOCK_MONOTONIC` at every join, which is what splits the
  window into `queue_lat` (submit → first GPU timestamp), GPU busy per phase,
  and `fence_tail` (last GPU timestamp → fence observed). `calmiss=0`,
  `qmiss=0` in every run.
- Accumulated per device and printed at exit as `[VKTS]` / `[VKTS+]` /
  `[VKTSROW]`; `prefill_profile.sh` folds them into its table as a row group
  under the CPU-side `eg` bucket.

Two derived counters matter more than the phase split:
`cpu_overlap` = issue → the engine coming back to join, and
**`gpu_late` = Σ max(0, last GPU timestamp − join entry): the only part of the
`eg` wait the GPU is responsible for.** Everything else in the window is the
engine's own deferred CPU experts running concurrently, by design (§G9).

### The gate (`~/bench/rp4_chain.sh`, pristine `~/bench/glm53.p6b` 53ccbb42…)

| step | result |
|---|---|
| `prefill_gate.sh` knob **OFF**, `MIN_SPEEDUP=0.97` | `teacher_forcing: IDENTICAL (782 positions)`, `logits: cosine=1.0000000 max_abs=0`, TTFT **0.99× / 1.00× / 1.00×** at 30/300/1000, decode 5.5/5.7 · 5.1/5.0 · 3.9/3.9 → **rc 0** |
| `tworeq.py` `TWOREQ_SLOTS=4`, `COLI_KDA_GPU=2` | `IDENTICAL across 4 requests`, **0 forcing lines** → rc 0 |
| `prefill_gate.sh` knob **ON** (`COLI_VK_TIMESTAMPS=1`), `MIN_SPEEDUP=0.97` | `teacher_forcing: IDENTICAL (782 positions)`, `logits: cosine=1.0000000 max_abs=0`, TTFT **1.00× / 1.00× / 1.00×**, decode unchanged → **rc 0** |
| four qwen38 C tests (`backend_vulkan.c` is shared) | prefix / config / metrics / serve_framing all exit 0 |

Prefill wall at the profile prompt is the same in all three knob positions:
**120.9 / 120.6 / 120.7 ms/token** at off / `=1` / `=2` (781 tokens), and
**174.3 / 174.1–174.5 / 174.3** at 3 462. The timestamps are free at both
levels. Served since 20:00 as `325c9c7a…`, serving script unchanged.

### `[VKTS]` — 600-token prompt (781 tokens), `COLI_KDA_GPU=2`, cap 512, chunk 128, three repeats

`TOTAL 120.9 / 120.6 / 120.6 ms/token · ffn_moe 65.4 · cpu experts 53.6 · eg 58.1`

| dev | chunks | experts | rows/exp | gate_up | down | **busy** | queue_lat | submit→fence | cpu_overlap | join_wait | **gpu_late** | busy % |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 294 | 6 582 | 9.32 | 1.55 | 0.42 | **1.97** | 0.07 | 57.2 | 57.2 | 0.01 | **0.000** (0/294) | 3.4 % |
| 2 | 319 | 8 585 | 5.05 | 0.55 | 0.38 | **0.93** | 0.25 | 54.9 | 54.9 | 0.07 | **0.063** (25/319) | 1.7 % |
| 3 | 305 | 8 207 | 4.82 | 0.52 | 0.37 | **0.89** | 0.21 | 54.9 | 54.9 | 0.01 | **0.005** (6–10/305) | 1.6 % |

ms per prompt token; medians of three repeats, which agree to < 2 %.
Summed GPU busy **3.78 / 3.80 / 3.81**, summed `gpu_late` **0.068 / 0.071 /
0.069 ms/token**.

### `[VKTS]` — 3 000-token prompt (3 462 tokens), same knobs, three repeats

`TOTAL 174.1 / 174.5 / 174.3 ms/token · ffn_moe 61.4 · cpu experts 51.6 · eg 54.2 · mla 90.4 (attn 69.6)`

| dev | chunks | experts | rows/exp | gate_up | down | **busy** | queue_lat | submit→fence | cpu_overlap | **gpu_late** | busy % |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 1 176 | 28 284 | 9.71 | 1.54 | 0.41 | **1.95** | 0.07 | 53.3 | 53.3 | **0.000** (0/1 176) | 3.6 % |
| 2 | 1 290 | 37 892 | 5.28 | 0.71 | 0.39 | **1.10** | 0.24 | 52.9 | 52.8 | **0.068** (115/1 290) | 2.1 % |
| 3 | 1 228 | 36 538 | 4.84 | 0.68 | 0.37 | **1.04** | 0.23 | 52.9 | 52.9 | **0.005** (37/1 228) | 2.0 % |

Summed GPU busy **4.10 / 4.07 / 4.10**, summed `gpu_late` **0.073 / 0.072 /
0.073 ms/token**.

### Where the 58 ms/token actually goes

`[PROF] eg` has contained the CPU expert time it overlaps since §G9 — the
record already says so in `profile_parse.py`'s docstring — but the P4b/P4c
notes read the inclusive number as GPU work. In numbers, at 781 tokens:

| | ms/token | share of `eg` |
|---|---:|---:|
| `eg` (the bucket P4b/P4c tried to move) | 58.1 | 100 % |
| — CPU experts running concurrently inside it | 53.6 | **92.3 %** |
| — everything else the group costs (`eg − cpu`) | 4.5 | 7.7 % |
| — — GPU busy, busiest device (dev0) | 1.97 | 3.4 %, fully hidden |
| — — **time the GPU made the engine wait (`gpu_late`)** | **0.068** | **0.12 %** |
| — — host side: 3 mallocs, x memcpy, 3×(descriptors + record + submit), fence syscalls, the weighted accumulate | ≈ 4.4 | 7.6 % |

At 3 462 tokens the same reading gives `eg` 54.2, CPU 51.6 (95.2 %),
`eg − cpu` 2.6, GPU busy 1.95, `gpu_late` 0.073 ms/token.

**A shader that ran in zero time would have saved 0.07 ms of a 120.6 ms
token.** P4b (0.91×) and P4c (1.03×) did not fail to move the expert group;
there was nothing there to move. Both remaining suspects are refuted directly:
per-dispatch fixed cost is inside the 1.95–1.97 ms/token of GPU busy and
`queue_lat` is 0.07–0.25 ms/token — both hidden — and occupancy cannot matter
for a unit that is idle 96–98 % of its own window.

### Per-expert GPU time and how it scales with rows (`COLI_VK_TIMESTAMPS=2`, 781 tokens)

Dispatches are serialized in this mode, so these are isolated per-dispatch
costs — and since level 2's phase totals equal level 1's, they are also what
the engine really pays.

| dev | rows | n | gate_up µs | down µs | total µs | µs/row |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 1 | 1 215 | 80.9 | 41.2 | **122.0** | 122.0 |
| 0 | 2 | 922 | 24.3 | 12.1 | **36.3** | 18.1 |
| 0 | 3–4 | 1 166 | 58.5 | 19.9 | 78.4 | 22.4 |
| 0 | 5–8 | 1 345 | 170.3 | 34.4 | 204.7 | 31.5 |
| 0 | 9–16 | 1 013 | 246.2 | 53.3 | 299.5 | 24.0 |
| 0 | 17–32 | 520 | 428.4 | 97.6 | 526.0 | 21.5 |
| 0 | 33–64 | 296 | 679.4 | 185.4 | 864.8 | 17.8 |
| 0 | 65+ | 105 | 1 071.4 | 335.1 | 1 406.5 | ≈ 21.8 |
| 2 | 1 | 1 875 | 43.6 | 41.7 | **85.2** | 85.2 |
| 2 | 2 | 1 461 | 19.1 | 12.5 | **31.6** | 15.8 |
| 2 | 3–4 | 2 012 | 29.9 | 18.2 | 48.1 | 13.7 |
| 2 | 5–8 | 2 018 | 58.4 | 34.2 | 92.5 | 14.2 |
| 2 | 9–16 | 916 | 81.9 | 57.1 | 139.0 | 11.1 |
| 2 | 17–32 | 227 | 146.3 | 106.6 | 252.8 | 10.3 |
| 2 | 33–64 | 64 | 301.9 | 213.2 | 515.1 | 10.6 |
| 3 | 1 | 1 970 | 42.7 | 42.1 | **84.7** | 84.7 |
| 3 | 2 | 1 428 | 18.2 | 12.8 | **31.0** | 15.5 |
| 3 | 5–8 | 1 817 | 63.9 | 34.2 | 98.1 | 15.1 |
| 3 | 33–64 | 58 | 290.1 | 220.8 | 510.9 | 10.5 |

Three things fall out.

1. **A one-row expert costs 2.7–3.4× a two-row expert.** `vk_tile_ok4`
   requires `S > 1`, so a single-row expert runs the *per-row* shader, which
   stages x in `shared float xsh[6144]` and reads it back at a stride of 8
   floats — the same LDS bank conflict §P4b diagnosed in its own staging.
   The tiled shader with `rows = 1` would predicate 7 of its 8 row slots off
   and read x as vec4 from global. One-row experts are **5.1 % of rows and
   16 % of GPU busy** (1 215·122.0 + 1 875·85.2 + 1 970·84.7 = 475 ms of a
   2 944 ms total).
2. **Above one row the cost is nearly linear in rows, not flat.** dev2 goes
   31.6 → 92.5 µs from 2 to 8 rows: 2.9× for 4× the rows. An R = 8 tile whose
   point is one weight read per 8 output rows should have been close to flat
   there. Marginal cost settles at **10.5 µs/row**.
3. **dev0 pays ~2× dev2/dev3 for the same shape** (5–8 rows: 204.7 vs 92.5 /
   98.1 µs). dev0 also runs the dense matmuls, KDA, MLA and the shared expert;
   its expert group queues behind them. That is a measured price for dev0's
   double duty.

### Why the tile is not flat in rows — the arithmetic

Shapes: hidden D = 4 096, moe_inter I = 2 048, int4 g64, 288 routed experts,
top-8, 42 MoE layers.

| per expert | bytes / flops | floor on dev0 |
|---|---:|---:|
| weights (gate+up+down, int4 + f32 group scales) | 14.16 MB, read **once** by the tile | 15.7 µs at ~900 GB/s |
| arithmetic, per activation row | 50.33 MFLOP | 0.84 µs at ~60 TFLOPS fp32 |
| **activations re-read, per activation row** | gate_up 2 048 output rows × 16 KB + down 4 096 × 8 KB = **67.1 MB** | **10.5 µs at ~6.4 TB/s** |

The measured marginal cost is 10.5 µs per row (dev2/dev3, large experts).
Against 0.84 µs of arithmetic and a weight stream that is paid once, the only
term that fits is the third: for every one of its O output rows the tile
re-reads the whole R × I activation tile out of L2 / Infinity Cache, so the
activation traffic is **67.1 MB per row** — 4.7× the entire weight stream, per
row — and 67.1 MB / 10.5 µs ≈ 6.4 TB/s is at the cache's own roof.

That is the real reason the S-tile did not make the expert group flat in rows,
and it also settles the P4b/P4c pair: **P4b's premise (get the activations out
of global memory) was right and its implementation was wrong** — it staged
R × I = 128 KB per workgroup and read the LDS at a conflicting stride — while
**P4c's premise (the loads are too many, not too large) was wrong**; vec4 cut
the instruction count and not one byte. Neither was ever going to show up in
the wall clock, because the unit was idle.

### Decode, for sizing only (`~/bench/rp4_decode.sh`, 21-token prompt, 64 greedy tokens, 5.97 tok/s)

Decode is the one regime where the GPU expert group is partly on the critical
path, because there is no 53 ms/token of CPU expert work to hide behind:

| | ms/token |
|---|---:|
| `eg` | 66.5 |
| — CPU experts inside it | 58.6 |
| — `eg − cpu` (matches §RP3's "GPU expert groups 8.43") | **8.0** |
| — — GPU busy dev0 / dev2 / dev3 | 10.63 / 7.94 / 6.45 (concurrent) |
| — — **`gpu_late`** dev0 / dev2 / dev3 | 1.01 / 0.77 / 0.18 → **1.96** |
| — — host side (residual) | ≈ 6.0 |

95–96 % of decode's expert dispatches are one-row, at 74.7 / 102.7 / 111.8 µs
against a two-row tiled dispatch's 61.6 / 38.6 / 43.7 µs — the same inversion,
now on 95 % of the work.

### What RP4 says to do, sized

1. **Nothing to the shader for prefill.** The ceiling on any expert-shader
   change is `gpu_late` = 0.07 ms of a 120.6 ms token (0.06 %). Refused here,
   not attempted.
2. **Allow the tile at `rows == 1`** (drop `S > 1` from `vk_tile_ok4`) is a
   real kernel finding worth ~2.5× on 95 % of decode's expert dispatches, but
   it is capped by decode's `gpu_late`: **≤ 1.96 ms of a 167.5 ms token,
   ≈ 1.2 %**, and `vk_tile_ok4` also gates the dense path, so the blast radius
   is all of decode and the gate is the decode track's, not this one's. Named
   for `ROADMAP-2026-09.md`, not executed here.
3. **The `eg − cpu` residual is host-side, and it is the larger number:**
   4.5 ms/token in prefill and ≈ 6.0 ms/token in decode (≈ 3.6 % of the decode
   token) for 126 issue/take pairs per token — ~48 µs of host cost per
   device-call. `VK_PROF=1` already splits memcpy / descriptors / record /
   submit; pointing it at this residual is the next profiling step, and §G13's
   "fuse the submits" is the shape of the fix.
4. **The prefill levers are unchanged and are not the GPU:** CPU experts
   53.6 ms/token (44 % of a 781-token prefill) and, at 3 462 tokens, MLA
   attention 69.6 ms/token (40 % of the token) — which is P5.

### Method notes

- **The gate caught its own contamination first.** The first attempt ran
  `prefill_gate.sh` with prefix checkpoints on: the pristine side runs first,
  writes an LCP checkpoint at ~381 tokens, and the candidate — which runs
  second — restores it, so size 300 read **45.5 s → 0.56 s** and the gate
  passed for that reason. `p6b_gate.sh` step 2 documents exactly this trap and
  turns `GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT=0`; `rp4_chain.sh` now does the same and points
  `COLI_CKPT_DIR` at its own directory, unsetting both before restarting the
  gateway so the chain's env cannot follow the owner's service. The numbers
  above are all from the re-run; the contaminated run is `rp4_chain_run2.log`.
- **`COLI_VK_TIMESTAMPS=2` is not free and says so.** It is the only mode that
  gives per-expert time, and the check that it is not lying is that its phase
  totals match level 1's to within 1 %.
- The `[VKTSROW]` line prints the open-ended top bin as `rows=65..64`; the
  parser renders it `65+`. Cosmetic, and left in the engine so the shipped
  binary is byte-for-byte the one the gate ran.


## P5 (2026-09-07 20:38 – 23:42) — the sequential remainder: 174.4 → 146.4 ms/token at 3 462 tokens, bit-identical, no knob needed

RP4 closed by naming the prefill levers that are not the GPU, and the biggest
was MLA attention: **69.6 ms/token at 3 462 tokens, 40 % of the prefill token**.
P5 is that, plus the two smaller sequential stages the roadmap listed with it.
Three sub-items, three knobs, gated together and attributed separately:

| | knob | what | where | verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **P5.1** | `COLI_MLA_HEADVEC` | the MLA attention score pass with the 64 heads in the SIMD lanes | CPU | **landed**, −23.2 ms/token at 3 462 |
| **P5.2** | `COLI_KDA_ROWS` | a chunk's S recurrence steps in ONE submit | GPU (`COLI_KDA_GPU != 0`) | **landed**, −2.2 ms/token |
| **P5.3** | `COLI_ROUTER_LANES` | the router dot with the chunk's tokens in the SIMD lanes | CPU | **landed**, −3.8 ms/token |

All three default ON, all three **bit-identical** (`max_abs=0` against the RP4
binary at 782 teacher-forced positions, at BOTH KDA knobs), all three
reversible from the environment — which is what makes the before/after table
below one binary at one temperature rather than two builds.

### One cause, three places: gcc vectorises the products and then adds them one at a time

`tools/hot-expert/rome_mlaattn.c` measures the MLA score loop in isolation at
the engine's own shapes (H = 64 heads, L = kv_lora 512, width = index_topk 2048
+ kpool 4 − 1 = 2051, 11 DSA layers) and finds it at **9.5–10.8 GMAC/s on eight
threads — 0.4 MAC/cycle/thread**, an order of magnitude under this box. The
reason is in the assembly of `dot += a[d]*b[d]`, and it is the same reason in
the router:

```
        vmulps  (%rdx,%rax), %ymm0, %ymm0     # gcc 15 DOES vectorise the products
        vaddss  %xmm0, %xmm2, %xmm2           # ... and then adds them one at a
        vaddss  %xmm2, %xmm4, %xmm4           #     time, in order, because it
        vaddss  %xmm4, %xmm2, %xmm2           #     may not reassociate floats
```

512 additions in ONE dependency chain at 3–5 cycles of latency each. The loop
is **latency-bound on its own accumulator** — not bandwidth-bound, not
ALU-bound. Nothing about the arithmetic has to change to fix it; it only has to
run in more chains at once, which means finding a free axis for the lanes:

- MLA attention: the **64 heads** share one latent row. Transpose the token's
  absorbed queries to `qT[d][h]` once (128 KB) and every head is a lane.
- the router: the **chunk's tokens** share one router row. Transpose `x` to
  `xT[d][t]` once per layer (2 MB at S = 128) and every token is a lane.

Lane j then accumulates over d in ascending order, one rounded product per
step: **the reference's arithmetic, unchanged, in 64 chains instead of one.**
The roadmap had budgeted the router as "order changes → not bit-identical → off
by default with the logit diff in the commit body". That is not necessary and
the knob defaults on.

**The FMA trap, recorded because it cost the first oracle run.** The reference
rounds each product before accumulating (`vmulps` then `vaddss`). Written with
intrinsics, `_mm256_add_ps(acc, _mm256_mul_ps(x, y))` is contracted straight
back into `vfmadd231ps`; `#pragma STDC FP_CONTRACT OFF` does **not** stop gcc
15 (verified: it still emits `vfmadd231ps`). The result is 1 ulp off on 85 % of
the values — `rome_mlaattn.c` reported **78 355 of 92 416 scores differing**.
An empty `__asm__("" : "+x"(m))` on the product forces the rounding and costs
no instruction. Anything that vectorises a colibri reduction on this box needs
that barrier or it is not bit-identical.

### The microbenchmark, and the honest ceiling it did NOT predict

`rome_mlaattn.c`, 8 threads, `OMP_PLACES=cores`, ms per prompt token over the
11 DSA layers (directly comparable to the engine's `mla.attn` bucket):

| `used` slots | which prompt | latent pool | score pass base | cand | transpose | speedup |
|---:|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 392 | 781 tok (mean) | 77 MB (11 layers) | 14.9 | 0.85 | 0.11 | **17.6×** |
| 1 444 | 3 462 tok (mean) | 7 MB (1 layer) | 48.3 | 2.26 | 0.11 | **20.4×** |
| 1 444 | 3 462 tok (mean) | 77 MB (11 layers) | 50.0 | 2.33 | 0.11 | **21.5×** |
| 2 048 | saturated | 77 MB | 72.2 | 3.23 | 0.11 | **21.6×** |

9.5–10.8 → 167–230 GMAC/s. **The 7 MB and the 77 MB pool give the same number**,
which answers "is this an L3 artefact": it is not, the op is compute-bound. (A
> 128 MB pool would have been dishonest rather than conservative here: the
engine's working set for this op is one layer's latent, 7.1 MB at 3 462 tokens,
78 MB for all eleven. The rule about outsizing L3 exists for weight streams.)

**Oracle: all 131 072 (head, slot) scores at the widest shape, bit for bit
against the scalar reference. 0 mismatches.**

And then the same harness measured the **whole** core — scores, softmax and the
weighted pool over the same rows — and that is the number that matched the
engine:

| `used` | whole core, base | whole core, cand | speedup | scores' share of base |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 392 | 16.8–17.0 | 11.1–11.4 | **1.47–1.53×** | 82–88 % |
| 1 444 | 66.9–67.0 | 41.2–42.5 | **1.58–1.63×** | 75–76 % |

The engine's `mla.attn` went 71.4 → 48.2 ms/token at 3 462: **1.48×**, next to
the harness's 1.58–1.63× for the same two phases. So a 21.5× score pass buys a
1.5× bucket, and the arithmetic says why: the candidate's scores cost 2.4 ms
where the baseline's cost 50, but the **softmax + weighted pool that follow
went from 17.0 to ~39 ms/token**. Separating the two passes costs about half of
what vectorising the first one saves.

**Why it is not the score read-back** — the obvious suspect, tested and
refuted. A third harness pass transposes `sc[u][h]` to `scT[h][u]` so the head
loop reads its scores contiguously: **41.96 vs 42.46 ms/token, i.e. nothing**.
What is left is the locality the old loop nest had by accident: for one head it
ran the dot over the selected latent rows and then immediately pooled the same
rows, so the pool's 2.9 MB was already warm. In the new nest the score pass
touches those rows once, on other threads, long before the pool walks them 64
times. **The weighted pool is now the largest term in the MLA bucket and it
walks the layer's latent once per head — 64 walks where one blocked rank-1
update over all 64 heads would do.** That is a real, sized, reproducible next
item (`P5b` in the roadmap) and not a P5 regression: the net is still 1.48× on
the bucket.

### The gate (`~/bench/p5_chain.sh` → `tools/hot-expert/p5_gate.sh`, rc 0 on all four steps)

prefill for everything they cannot cache.

### Two things the gate saw that are worth writing down

**1. At `COLI_KDA_GPU=0` — not the serving knob — `kda.step` doubles.** In step
a's profile the pristine's CPU recurrence is 6.6 ms/token and the candidate's
is 12.3. Nothing in P5 touches the CPU recurrence; what changed is the memory
around it. The CPU KDA state is 136 MiB against a 128 MB L3 (§"A cliff worth
recording") and P5 adds per-layer-per-chunk scratch that competes for it: `sc`
525 KB and `qT` 128 KB in MLA, `xT` 2 MB in the router. At the serving knob the
state lives on the device and the same column reads **5.7 → 3.3**. The CPU-knob
run is still net faster overall (121.6 → 118.4 ms/token), so no gate fires, but
anyone benchmarking at `COLI_KDA_GPU=0` should expect this bucket to move the
wrong way.

**2. The candidate's 1 230-token serve row moved 12 % between step a and step
a2, and it is not explained.** 136.6/136.4 s in step a, 153.5/153.1 s in step
a2, forty minutes apart, same binary, same nominal configuration; the PRISTINE
rows in the same two steps agree to 1 % (171.0/170.9 vs 169.3/169.0). Checked
and ruled out: residency 100.0000 % in both, expert-cache hit rate 100.0 % in
every row of both, `majflt` identical row for row (3/0/5/0/1/0). Both readings
pass the gate and both are printed above rather than averaged; the per-bucket
profile (three repeats, fixed conditions, single binary) is the tighter
instrument and says 1.19× at 3 462 tokens and 1.10× at 781.

### What P5.2 actually removed, and what is left in it

`kda_step.comp` gained a `tok` push constant that selects the dispatch's slice
of the chunk's `qkv`/`gate`/`beta`/`out`; every single-token caller leaves it 0
and gets the indices it had, so `coli_vk_kda_layer` (the G12 decode chain) is
untouched. `coli_vk_kda_step_rows` records S dispatches into one command buffer
with a compute barrier between consecutive tokens — write→read **and**
write→write, because the state is read-modify-written — so the recurrence stays
sequential in t and only the S−1 extra submit+fence round trips go.

Measured: **6.0 → 3.8 ms/token, −38 %.** Not the "most of the submit overhead"
the roadmap projected, and the residual says why: at 34 KDA layers per token
the batched path still memcpys 3 × 32 KB of q/k/v plus 32 KB of gate into
write-combined device memory and 32 KB of `out` back, ≈ 5.5 MB per token, which
at this box's host→device rate is most of the 3.8 ms that is left. The submits
themselves are gone (128 per layer per chunk → 1); the copies are not, and
staging q/k/v on the device instead of on the host is the shape of the next
step for this bucket.

### Method notes

- `~/bench/p5_chain.sh` runs the whole thing unattended in `p6b_chain.sh`'s
  structure, with one addition the earlier chains did not need: **the `.spv`
  files are gitignored, so `git reset --hard` does not revert a shader.** The
  revert path copies `~/bench/shaders_p5base/*.spv` over `c/shaders` explicitly
  and verifies the `kda_step.spv` sha before restarting the gateway.
- The chain's live-row step (e) was refused the first time — `ttft_serve.py
  --url` without `--warm` at 91.68 % residency — and was re-run by hand against
  the serving gateway. Add `--warm` to that step in the next chain.
- The profile block runs BEFORE the gate deliberately: the smoke (30 tokens,
  teacher forcing against both the knobs-off configuration and the pristine
  binary) is the cheap correctness filter, and running the profiles first means
  a failing gate still leaves the diagnosis in the record.

## Upstream dev merge (2026-09-08 00:07 – 01:22) — `JustVugg/colibri` `dev` 1ccee43 into `hot-expert-tier`, bit-identical, neutral on every row

The housekeeping item at the bottom of `PREFILL-ROADMAP-2026-09.md`. Merge base
`12a5c46`; the fork was **149 ahead / 125 behind**. `~/bench/devmerge_chain.sh`
ran it unattended in `p5_chain.sh`'s structure (stop gateway → `--ff-only`
merge of `p0-sync` → build → tests → five gate steps → serve only on rc 0 →
live check → verified sha256 revert on any failure → gateway restarted on every
exit path). Pristine `~/bench/glm53.p5` (`5dd26f65…`), candidate `5ed096a6…`,
shaders `~/bench/shaders_p5`. **In service since 01:11, serving script
unchanged.**

### What conflicted, and the one place the resolution is not "take dev's"

Three files, eight hunks — the file list the 2026-09-07 dry run predicted, three
more hunks than it did. Details and the full resolution table are in
`DEV-MERGE-NOTE-2026-09-07.md`. The mapping hunks are the item's point:
`dev`'s #1325 (`st.h::st_map_shard_range`, the fork's own PR upstreamed as
`eabeb9a`) replaces the fork's private `g_fmap[]` in `glm53.c` and
`q38_shard_*` in `qwen38_core.h`. Taken literally it would also have turned the
mapping **off** (upstream ships it opt-in behind `COLI_MAP_EXPERTS=1`), dropped
`g_map_all` — which `expert_cache_init` reads to size the LRU at one slot per
expert — dropped both prefaults, and left `st.h`'s unsynchronised per-fd table
to be filled from inside glm53's OpenMP region. So: `st.h` gained
`COLI_MAP_EXPERTS_DEFAULT` (0 unless an engine defines it, env still wins both
ways), `glm53.c` and `qwen38.c` define it to 1, and `expert_map_init` stays,
reimplemented over the shared primitive. That is the direction upstream is
going anyway — #1350's commit message calls itself "preparazione al flip del
default di `COLI_MAP_EXPERTS`".

### Step 1 — tests

| test | result |
|---|---|
| `tests/test_qwen38_prefix` | exit 0 — single QSA allocation, exact/extension reuse, reset |
| `tests/test_qwen38_config` | exit 0 |
| `tests/test_qwen38_metrics` | exit 0 |
| `tests/test_qwen38_serve_framing` | exit 0 |
| `tests/test_glm53_chat_template.py --template <SNAP>/chat_template.jinja` | PASS — **25 renders byte-identical to `chat_template.jinja`**, tools and reasoning levels included |
| `make test-python` (`unittest discover -s tests`) | **790 tests OK** (74 skipped), 74 s |

The `ensure_kv` guard that fixed `test_qwen38_prefix`'s SIGFPE survives the
merge. The nine `.spv` files rebuilt **byte-identical** to `~/bench/shaders_p5`:
`dev` touches no shader, no `backend_vulkan.c`, no `quant.h`, no
`sparse_index.h`.

### Step 2 — the mapping change, which is the only thing here that could move memory

Both binaries, same 30-token prompt, `MemAvailable` sampled every 5 s and the
**floor** taken:

| | pristine `glm53.p5` | candidate (merged) |
|---|---|---|
| `[MAP]` | `59 file mappati, esperti mappabili 12096/12096 (tutti: nessuna copia)` | `59 file mappati (st_map_shard_range), esperti mappabili 12096/12096 (tutti: nessuna copia)` |
| `[PROF] mmap` | `serve=7446 copy=0` | `serve=7446 copy=0` |
| binds | `gpu=1645 cpu=5801 (contig=0 split=5801)` | `gpu=1645 cpu=5801 (contig=0 split=5801)` |
| expert cache | 288 slots/layer × 42 sparse layers | 288 slots/layer × 42 sparse layers |
| budget line | `44.3 GB (247.3 totali, 183.2 modello, 19.8 margine)` | `44.3 GB (247.3 totali, 183.2 modello, 19.8 margine)` |
| MemAvailable floor | **209 GB** | **221 GB** |
| load / prefill | 89.5 s / 15 tok in 3.8 s | 89.5 s / 15 tok in 3.8 s |

`copy=0` on both sides is the number that matters: every expert is served from
the mapping, nothing is `pread` into anonymous memory, and the LRU is still
sized at one slot per expert. The candidate's floor is 12 GB **higher**, not
lower — it ran second, on a warmer cache (its own budget line reports 220.4 GB
available at load against the pristine's 209.8). `majflt` per request stays at
**0** through the whole gate on both binaries (3–5 on the very first request of
a fresh engine, identically on both). After the merged engine had been serving
for ten minutes: `used 15 GB, buff/cache 184 GB, available 231 GB`.

```

logits: cosine=1.0000000 max_abs=0 argmax 7664 vs 7664 OK
```

**`max_abs=0` — bit-identical, as a merge that changes no arithmetic must be.**

| size | pristine ttft (both runs) | candidate ttft (both runs) | speedup | decode p/c |
|---:|---|---|---:|---|
| 30 | 6.8 s / 2.6 s | 6.9 s / 2.6 s | 0.99× | 5.3 / 5.4 |
| 300 | 42.8 s / 42.2 s | 43.0 s / 42.4 s | 1.00× | 5.5 / 5.6 |
| 1000 | 152.5 s / 152.7 s | 153.5 s / 155.5 s | 0.99× | 4.5 / 4.4 |

`speedup gate: PASS`, gate `rc=0`. Neutral at every size, decode within 10 %.

**One number that does not agree with that, recorded because it does not:** the
gate's own CLI teacher-forcing run (`--greedy 0`, 781 tokens, the oracle half)
put the candidate at **108.5 ms/token against the pristine's 117.0**, and the
whole difference is `mla.attn` **22.4 → 14.2 ms/token**; every other bucket is
within 0.1. Nothing in the merge touches that loop, and the candidate's 108.5
is what §P5 already recorded for the P5 binary at 781 tokens (109.2) — so it is
the *pristine's* single run that is the outlier, not the candidate's. The
plausible mechanism is code layout: `dev`'s 900ce44 marks `now_s()`
`__attribute__((noinline))` (GCC 16 ICE on MSYS2), which changes GCC's IPA
inline budget across the whole translation unit. **The serving regime says
neutral at three sizes with two repeats each, and that is the number this
record keeps.** No tuning was done toward the favourable one.

### Step 6 — `rome_bench.sh` glm53, paired in one session

The roadmap's stated gate. The last recorded glm53 rows are from **2026-09-05**,
before P2…P7, so "reproduce its last rows" can only be honest as a **pair**:
the P5 binary and the merged one, back to back, same session, same histogram

Rotating and cold reproduce the 2026-09-05 rows inside 3 %. The
warm-identical column is 5–6 % low **on both binaries equally**, so it is not
the merge; it is an n = 1 upper-bound row taken three days and five landed
items later. `rome_bench.sh`'s own `sudo -n … drop_caches` fails on this box
(no passwordless sudo) and says so, but the cold row is still a real cold row:
`datapoint.py`'s `evict_cache` drops the shards with
`posix_fadvise(POSIX_FADV_DONTNEED)`, no privileges needed, and the table
labels it `cold request (cache evicted before engine load)`.

### What upstream changed that matters on this rig

1. **#1325 / `eabeb9a` — `st.h::st_map_shard_range`.** The conflict; resolved
   above. Note for `CLAUDE.md`: `eabeb9a` is now an ancestor of
   `hot-expert-tier`, and it is *not* the commit that thrashed this box.
2. **#1327 + #1278 (`53c57f1`, `2ef8dc5`) — the GLM-5.3 prompt changes.**
   `render_chat_glm53` now always emits `<|system|>Reasoning Effort: {Low|High|Max}`
   and always ends `<|assistant|><think>`; `starts_in_reasoning()` returns True
   for `glm53` whatever the client's flag says. Two consequences here:
   (a) the served token prefix changes, so a P7 checkpoint stored under the old
   rendering **misses once** and is re-captured (cheap today — Open WebUI's
   builtin tools are off, so the prefix is the system message);
   (b) GLM-5.3's reasoning now arrives in the thinking channel instead of glued
   in front of the answer. The chat-template test pins the new form at 25
   renders.
3. **#1350 (`1e87fb3`) — RSS counts owned memory.** `current_rss_gb` reads
   `RssAnon` instead of `VmRSS` — **in `colibri.c`**, the engine whose
   `rss_guard` evicts experts. `glm53.c` has no `rss_guard` and reports
   `ru_maxrss` in its `STAT` line, so glm53 is unaffected. Its importance here
   is that it is upstream's stated precondition for defaulting the mapping on,
   which is what this merge does per-engine.
4. **#1375 (`ec6656e`) — `memory_available_gb` → `compat_mem_available_gb()`**
   in `glm53.c`, plus a real message on the slot-allocation OOM. On Linux the
   value is unchanged: the budget line reads 44.3 GB on both binaries.
5. **`822a5c0` + `dda96f2`/`a6fd3c2`/`900ce44` — glm53 Brain and Profile.** An
   `EMAP` line after `READY`, `HITS` and a 9-field `PROF` after every turn,
   `ehit_mark` per routed expert, and `m->t_attn/t_ffn/t_disk/t_head/forwards`
   wall clocks that run unconditionally (the fork's `optime` ones are behind
   `COLI_TIMERS`). `openai_server.Engine` parses all three and every harness
   here goes through it (`ttft_serve.py`, `tworeq.py`, `datapoint.py`), so
   nothing had to change; `Engine.profile` is a bounded deque. `900ce44` also
   marks `now_s()` noinline — see the `mla.attn` note in step 3.
6. **#1283 (`b94d819`) — glm53 `max_kv_slots` 1 → 16 upstream**, independently
   reaching P6's value. The number was never in conflict; only the fork's
   comment above it was.
7. **#1376 (`af3e7cf`) — the context ceiling.** `CONTEXT_EXCEEDED` is now read
   by key (`capacity`) instead of by position, and `coli --ctx` above a
   family's ceiling is refused with the number instead of silently clamped.
8. **#1354 (`2201ee2`) — `image_url` local reads confined** (`..` refused,
   `COLI_IMAGE_ROOT`, no path in the error). This gateway binds 0.0.0.0, so it
   is a real fix here.
9. **Makefile (#1284, `a40384d`, `0de98d7`, `59fd240`)** — per-engine header
   prerequisites and an engine list derived from the Makefile, with
   `test_makefile_deps.py` to keep it honest. Practical effect: touching
   `st.h`, `quant.h` or `compat.h` now actually rebuilds the engines that
   include them, which is the failure mode CLAUDE.md's "a shared-file change
   must rebuild both engines" rule exists for.
10. **#1332 (`25ecb13`) — CANCEL during a turn, `serve_poll.h`.** Wired for
    `qwen36` only; `glm53` still answers `NOT_FOUND`, so no change here — but
    it is the pattern glm53 will follow.
11. **#1321 (cache budget) is NOT in `dev`** and is still open. The fork
    already carries its own model-aware budget (`d9d38c5`), which is what the
    44.3 GB line above comes from.
12. Everything else is `qwen36`/CUDA tier work (#687, #1334, #1339–#1341,
    #1344, `COLI_PLACE=auto`, fp8 streaming), `deepseek_v4` REAP-150B support,
    the web dashboard and CI. None of it runs on this box. `deepseek_v4.c`
    moved 573 lines but touched **no** `v4_ckpt_*` symbol, so P7's port has not
    drifted from its source.

### Method notes

- **The qwen38 mapping path is compiled and unit-tested, not measured.** The
  four C tests cover it; running `qwen38` on its 173 GiB model would have
  evicted GLM from the page cache, and this session was told not to. The
  Q-track's `qwen38-vk` rows (`q-baseline`, 2026-09-06) should be re-taken
  the next time Qwen is warmed, as the first item of any Qwen work.
- `~/bench/devmerge_chain.sh` extracts `rome_bench.sh`'s remote body verbatim
  so the regime is the recorded one, and only redirects the destination of the
  mechanical datapoint row — the rig's tree must stay clean for the
  fast-forward. The redirect silently produced no file (the harness only
  appends to a record that already exists); the rows above were read from the
  harness's own `RESULT:` and summary tables instead.
- The chain's `memfloor` step runs BEFORE anything long, deliberately: the one
  failure this merge could cause is model bytes in anonymous memory, and it
  costs 8 minutes to rule out instead of 3 hours.
- New pristines for the next item: `~/bench/glm53.devmerge` (`5ed096a6…`) and
  `~/bench/shaders_devmerge`.

## P5b (2026-09-08 06:00 – 21:24) — the MLA weighted pool: the big term was FALSE SHARING on the per-thread scratch, not the 64 walks

The roadmap sized P5b from P5's own harness: *"the softmax + weighted pool that
follow went 17.0 → ~39 ms/token at `used` = 1 444 … the pool walks the layer's
latent once per head — 64 walks where one blocked rank-1 update over all 64
heads would do."* The mechanism it names is real and is built here, behind
`COLI_MLA_POOL=2`. **It is not the reason for the 39 ms, and in the engine it
is worth nothing.** The reason is that the per-thread scratch shares cache
lines with its neighbours.

`mla_layer` sized its per-thread slices as

```c
float *pooled_pool = malloc((size_t)nthreads_mla * L * sizeof(float));      /* L     =  512 ->  2 048 B */
float *score_pool  = malloc((size_t)nthreads_mla * width * sizeof(float));  /* width = 2051 ->  8 204 B */
```

glibc returns a 16-byte-aligned pointer and 8 204 is not a multiple of 64, so
every pair of neighbouring threads shares the boundary cache lines of their
`pooled` and `score` slices — and the pool writes `pooled[0..L)` once per
selected slot, i.e. it writes those shared lines `used` = 1 444 times per head
per layer. Eight threads ping-pong two lines each, continuously, for the whole
bucket. **`mla.attn` 51.6 → 22.5 ms/token at 3 462 tokens from rounding two
allocations up to a cache line.**

### The microbenchmark, extended (`tools/hot-expert/rome_mlaattn.c`)

Two additions: the blocked pool with its own exhaustive oracle, and a `pad`
argument that switches the per-thread slices between the engine's shape and a
64-byte-aligned, cache-line-padded one (`pad=0` offsets the base by 4 floats so
the misalignment is deterministic instead of whatever the allocator did that
day). 8 threads, `OMP_PLACES=cores`, latent pool 24 slabs = **169 MB,
deliberately over this box's 128 MB L3**, ms per prompt token over the 11 DSA
layers, medians of three repeats. These are cache-warm numbers: the harness is
CPU-only, has no engine around it, and the residency check does not apply to it.

| pass | used = 392, engine's scratch | used = 392, padded | used = 1 444, engine's scratch | used = 1 444, padded |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| core, pre-P5 nest | 17.29 | 15.31 | 64.59 | 57.77 |
| core, P5 (the code in service) | 12.10 | 4.64 | **44.75** | **14.76** |
| core, P5 + `sc` transposed to `scT[h][u]` | 12.14 | 4.51 | 44.95 | 14.86 |
| core, P5b blocked pool | **4.11** | 4.09 | **11.76** | 12.29 |
| — of which the pool alone | 0.99 | 1.03 | 4.48 | 4.89 |

Read the second and fourth columns against each other: **padding the slices,
changing nothing else, takes the whole core from 44.75 to 14.76 ms/token at
`used` = 1 444** — 30 of the 39 ms the roadmap attributed to lost locality.
The blocked pool is worth ~2.5 ms more on top of the padding at `used` = 1 444
and nothing at `used` = 392, and it is immune to the artefact by construction:
its accumulator planes are per d-tile, 2 KB apart, never sharing a line, which
is why its column barely moves between padded and unpadded.

The score pass is untouched and still 20–22× (2.3 ms/token at `used` = 1 444,
227 GMAC/s = 96 % of this box's two-vector-FP-ops-per-cycle ceiling). **The
strided score read-back stays refuted**: `scT[h][u]` is within noise in all
twelve runs, exactly as §P5 found.

`d`-tile chosen by measurement (the pool alone, ms/token): 8 / 16 / 32 / 64 give
4.13 / 4.17 / 4.02 / 6.39 at `used` = 1 444 and 0.96 / 1.00 / 1.47 / 1.91 at
`used` = 392. 8 is the only one that wins at both and is `GLM53_POOL_DTILE`.

### Numerics: the pool's reference term is NOT the score pass's

The score reduction cannot vectorise, so gcc emits `vmulps` for the products and
an in-order `vaddss` chain — every product is rounded before it is added, which
is what P5's `MLA_MULADD` empty-`asm` barrier exists to reproduce. The pool is
elementwise, and gcc 15 `-O3 -march=native` **contracts** `pooled[d] += w *
c_j[d]` into `vfmadd213ps` (`-ffp-contract=fast` is the GNU default; confirmed
in the objdump of a standalone extract and of `mla_layer` itself). So the pool's
reference term rounds **once**, and a bit-identical blocked kernel must **use**
fma and must **not** add the barrier. It keeps `u` ascending for every `(h, d)`.
Anyone vectorising a colibri reduction needs the barrier; anyone vectorising a
colibri elementwise accumulate needs the opposite. Both facts now have a test.

Harness oracles: 92 416 (head, slot) scores and **32 768 pooled values (64 heads
× 512), at dtile 8 and dtile 32, 0 mismatches — BIT-IDENTICAL.**

### The per-bucket tables, serving knob, one binary, three knob settings

`COLI_KDA_GPU=2`, chunk 128, cap 512, `GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT=0`, ms per prompt
token, medians of the repeats in brackets. "pristine" is `~/bench/glm53.devmerge`
(`5ed096a6…`, the binary in service); `m0` is the SAME candidate binary with
`COLI_MLA_POOL=0`, which is the code P5 left behind.

**781 tokens (the 600-token prompt)**

| bucket | pristine | m0 (knob off) | m1 (padded) | m2 (+blocked pool) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| **TOTAL prefill** | 110.2 (110.0/110.5) | 108.7 (108.7/108.2/108.7) | **99.2** (99.2/99.2/99.2) | 99.4 (99.4/99.4/99.2) |
| layers | 107.5 | 106.0 | 96.5 | 96.6 |
| ffn_moe | 61.3 | 61.2 | 61.2 | 61.4 |
| — cpu experts | 53.5 | 53.5 | 53.4 | 53.6 |
| — eg (gpu) | 58.0 | 58.0 | 57.9 | 58.1 |
| — router | 1.4 | 1.4 | 1.4 | 1.4 |
| — shared | 1.5 | 1.5 | 1.5 | 1.5 |
| kda | 8.8 | 8.8 | 8.8 | 8.7 |
| — kda.step | 3.4 | 3.4 | 3.4 | 3.4 |
| mla | 30.8 | 29.2 | 19.9 | 19.9 |
| — **mla.attn** | **24.6** (24.5/24.8) | 23.0 (23.1/22.7/23.0) | **13.7** (13.8/13.6/13.7) | 13.7 (13.6/13.7/13.8) |
| hc+norm | 4.6 | 4.6 | 4.6 | 4.6 |
| head | 2.3 | 2.3 | 2.3 | 2.3 |
| **vs pristine** | 1.00× | 1.01× | **1.11×** | 1.11× |

**3 462 tokens (the 3 000-token prompt) — the one P5b is for**

| bucket | pristine | m0 (knob off) | m1 (padded) | m2 (+blocked pool) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| **TOTAL prefill** | 153.1 | 147.3 (146.2/147.5/147.3) | 118.1 (117.9/118.3) | **117.8** (117.9/117.8/117.4) |
| — **mla.attn** | **57.4** | 51.6 (50.4/51.8/51.6) | 22.5 (22.3/22.7) | **22.3** (22.3/22.2/22.3) |
| **vs pristine** | 1.00× | 1.04× | 1.30× | **1.30×** |
| **vs m0** | — | 1.00× | 1.25× | **1.25×** |

Three things these tables say.

**1. The padding is the entire item.** `m0 → m1` is `mla.attn` 51.6 → 22.5 at
3 462 tokens and 23.0 → 13.7 at 781, TOTAL 1.25× and 1.10×. Nothing else moves:
CPU experts 53.5, `eg` 58.0, `kda.step` 3.4 are identical to three digits in
every column, which is what licenses reading the difference as MLA's.

**2. The blocked pool — the mechanism the roadmap row is named after — is
neutral in the engine.** `m1 → m2` is +0.2 ms/token at 781 and −0.3 at 3 462
(`mla.attn` 22.5 → 22.3). Once the false sharing is gone, the per-head pool runs
at L3 bandwidth: one layer's latent is 7.1 MB at 3 462 tokens and all eleven are
78 MB, so the 180 MB the per-head form reads per token per layer is 2.8 MB of
distinct data read 64 times **out of L3**, and it costs about what reading it
once costs. The blocked form's advantage is real only when the latent stops
fitting — a projection, not a measurement. So the knob's default is **1**, per
the roadmap's own rule ("default on only if bit-identical AND faster at both
prompt sizes"), and mode 2 keeps its number instead of the default. It is not
deleted because §P5b's table is measured through it and because it is the shape
that wins above ~16k tokens of context.

**3. The artefact is an allocator lottery, and it explains the dev merge's one
recorded contradiction.** The pristine binary and the candidate's `m0` are the
same source for this code, yet `mla.attn` reads 57.4 against 51.6 at 3 462
tokens and 24.6 against 23.0 at 781. Nothing separates them but where `malloc`
put two buffers, which any relink can change. §"Upstream dev merge" recorded
exactly this shape as a contradiction it could not explain — *"the CLI oracle
run put the candidate 7 % faster with all of it in `mla.attn`, which nothing in
the merge touches"*. That is this bug: the merge moved the allocations, not the
arithmetic. With `COLI_MLA_POOL=1` the number stops depending on the link order.

P5b changes no shader, and the gate refuses if any `.spv` differs from the
pristine set — the inverse of p5_gate.sh's check, because here a difference
would mean something rebuilt a shader P5b has no business touching. All nine
`.spv` files compared equal in both runs.
