### Method notes

- **The Mac lost the rig's network at 10:00, an hour into run 1's 3 000-token
  profiles.** The chain is `setsid nohup`'d and restores the gateway on every
  exit path, so it ran the remaining nine profiles, both `prefill_gate` steps,
  `tworeq`, the checkpoint step, and then — on rc 3 — reverted to
  `~/bench/glm53.devmerge`, verified the binary and shader shas, and restarted
  the gateway, all unattended. Nothing was lost and nothing had to be redone.
  This is the property `p*_chain.sh` is written for; it is the first time it has
  actually been needed.
- Run 2 skipped the per-bucket profile block (`P5B_PROFILES=0`): all twenty
  profiles above come from run 1 on the same tree, every one of them setting
  `COLI_MLA_POOL` explicitly on the command line, and the only source change
  between the two binaries is the **default** value that those rows override.
- The harness's own artefact was found the same way the engine's was: the first
  extended `rome_mlaattn.c` reported `core cand` 16.4 ms/token where the
  committed harness reported 41.4 for the identical pass. The difference was
  one `malloc` becoming an `aligned_alloc` in the edit. That accident is the
  whole item; it is now a switch in the harness (`pad`) rather than luck.

## CANCEL (2026-09-08 21:46 – 2026-09-09 00:59) — glm53 honours CANCEL mid-turn, and the live path is where the design was wrong

The open dependency of the prefill track, measured 2026-09-06 and unfixed since:
a 1 230-token prompt, CANCEL at 5.0 s, the engine confirmed after **251.3 s** —
it ran the whole prefill and the whole generation. The gateway holds the
scheduler admission until the ack (`openai_server.py`, `generate()`), so a
client that disconnects frees nothing and every request behind it waits out a
turn nobody wants. The G16 attempt shipped broken because it was validated on
`--greedy`, which never executes `serve_one`.

Engine (`c/glm53.c`): `serve_poll.h` (#1332, the pattern `qwen36` already used)
polled **between prefill chunks** and **between decode steps** — never inside a
chunk, so the session is always at a position that exists. A cancel in the
prefill stops there and the slot remembers exactly `session->filled`; a cancel
in the decode stops after the current token. The answer is `PROF`, then
`DONE … STAT`, then `ERROR <id> CANCELLED`. Knobs: `GLM53_NO_CANCEL_POLL=1`
(off, default on), `GLM53_VERBOSE` prints `CANCEL <id> at prefill pos=P/T` or
`at decode tok=N`. Gate `cancel_gate.sh`, chain `cancel_chain.sh`, pristine
`~/bench/glm53.p5b` (65d7d70d…), candidate **f41fc5cc…**, four chain runs.

### The two things the measurements taught that the plan did not know

**1. A SUBMIT *can* arrive mid-turn, and it is not a corner case.**
`GenerationScheduler` is built with `capacity = kv_slots` (`openai_server.py`),
i.e. **four** in service, and `generate()` writes its frame as soon as it is
admitted. So the gateway can have four SUBMIT frames in one pipe; the engine
serves one and the rest wait in the tube — that *is* this engine's concurrency.
The first design read such a header, pushed it back and stopped draining (the
frames are byte-counted, so discarding a header would desync the parser), and
this file was going to record "a CANCEL behind a queued SUBMIT is not seen
until the turn ends — never worse than before". The live check said that is the
NORMAL case: the client hangs up, the next request is admitted on another slot
and its SUBMIT reaches the pipe first, the CANCEL lands behind it.

| live `curl -m 8`, gated binary `fb795eb1` (push-back only) | prompt | reused | ttft | gen | total |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| req 4 — the abandoned turn | 1 428 | 388 | 132.32 s | 46 | **132.32 s** |
| req 5 — the short request behind it | 15 | 0 | 125.96 s | 2 | **126.35 s** |

Twice out of two, with no `CANCEL … at …` line at all. One round earlier in the
same series the race went the other way and the same check read 7.00 s. **A fix
whose verdict depends on winning a millisecond race is not a fix.** The drain
now reads the queued frame in full (`serve_read_body`, the parser
`serve_read_req` already used) onto a small FIFO and keeps reading, so the
CANCEL behind it is seen; `serve_loop` serves that queue before stdin, in order.
The blocking body read is argued rather than assumed: `serve_poll.h` forbids
waiting for a command that may never come, and here the header is in hand and
`generate()` writes header+payload in one locked write plus flush, so the wait
is bounded by the writer — the same wait `serve_read_req` does for every
request, one place earlier. IMAGE frames are still pushed back (the engine holds
one announced image at a time).

**2. A cancelled request had never been logged at all.** `generate()`'s "done"
branch raises `ClientCancelled` *before* its `[req]` write, so
`owui_report.sh` — which reads exactly those lines — listed 1, 3, 5, 6, 8, 10,
12 and was missing 2, 4, 7, 9, 11: every cancelled one. Fixed in
`openai_server.py` (`req_log_line`, outcome `cancelled`), which is also what
made finding (1) visible. The engine's frame ORDER is what makes that line
carry numbers: the `ERROR` line pops `pending`, so a DONE behind it would be
dropped; with the DONE first, the log line has the prompt tokens the cancel
saved and the tokens already generated.

### The gate (run 4, tag `cancel09082357`, all four steps rc=0)

**Step a — `prefill_gate.sh`, `MIN_SPEEDUP=0.97`, `GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT=0`.** The
pristine has no poll at all, so these rows ARE the cost of one `select(2)` per
128-token chunk and per generated token. `teacher_forcing: IDENTICAL (782
positions)`, `logits: cosine=1.0000000 max_abs=0 argmax 7664 vs 7664` —
bit-identical, not merely close.

| size | pristine ttft (both runs) | candidate ttft (both runs) | speedup | decode p/c |
|---:|---|---|---:|---|
| 30 | 2.8 s / 2.6 s | 2.7 s / 2.5 s | **1.02×** | 5.4 / 5.5 |
| 300 | 40.2 s / 39.5 s | 40.3 s / 39.8 s | **0.99×** | 5.7 / 5.7 |
| 1000 | 136.7 s / 136.5 s | 138.5 s / 138.6 s | **0.99×** | 4.7 / 4.6 |

The poll is free. (Run 3 of the chain, same source for these paths, read
1.00×/1.00×/1.00×; the spread between the two is the allocator lottery §P5b
recorded, not the poll.)

**Step b — the cancel, in both phases**, each reproduced in two chain runs:

| phase | sent | engine confirmed | where it stopped | next request |
|---|---|---:|---|---:|
| prefill (1 236 tokens) | 5.0 s after SUBMIT | **12.9 s** / 12.9 s | `CANCEL 2 at prefill pos=128/1236` | 2.68 s / 2.72 s |
| decode (27 tokens, 256 asked) | 10.0 s after the FIRST TOKEN | **13.9 s** / 14.0 s | `CANCEL 2 at decode tok=56` | 2.64 s / 2.69 s |

251.3 s → 12.9 s. The ~8 s over the 5 s mark is the in-flight 128-token chunk
finishing, which is the atomicity the design pays for on purpose.

**Step c — a cancelled prefill resumes, and the resumed state is the same
state.** Cancel the 1 230-token prompt at 30 s, then submit it again:

| | prompt | reused | ttft |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| reference, never cancelled | 1 236 | 0 | 140.01 s / 138.88 s |
| cancelled at 30 s | — | — | confirmed 38.9 s / 38.7 s, `CANCEL 3 at prefill pos=384/1236` |
| the identical prompt, resubmitted | 1 236 | **384** | **99.73 s / 97.49 s** |

`REUSE` equals the position the cancel reported, exactly; greedy text
**IDENTICAL** to the uncancelled run (89 bytes, 24 tokens, both). 99.73/140.01 =
0.71 and 384/1236 = 0.31 — the time saved is the fraction resumed, which is what
"resumable" has to mean. This is the oracle that the partial state is *correct*;
no timing can give it.

**Step d — the rest of the serve path is untouched.** `tworeq.py` at 4 slots:
`IDENTICAL across 4 requests` at `COLI_KDA_GPU=2` and at `=0`, **0 forcing
lines**. P7 capture/restore: turn 1 `REUSE 0/1540`, turn 2 `REUSE 1527/1544`,
after an engine restart `REUSE 1527`, **t1/t2 = 78.81×**.

### Live, through the gateway (binary f41fc5cc… in service)

| request | prompt | reused | ttft | gen | outcome |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| e1, cancelled 5 s after the first token | 1 248 | 388 | 92.97 s | 25 | **cancelled**, `CANCEL 2 at decode tok=25` |
| the next request | 27 | 0 | **2.61 s** | 8 | length |
| `curl -m 8` round 1 | 1 428 | 388 | — | 0 | **cancelled at 13.18 s**, `CANCEL 4 at prefill pos=516/1428` |
| the short request behind it | 15 | 0 | 6.79 s | 2 | **7.13 s** |
| `curl -m 8` round 2 | 1 428 | **516** | — | 0 | **cancelled at 14.24 s**, `CANCEL 6 at prefill pos=644/1428` |
| the short request behind it | 15 | 0 | 7.86 s | 2 | **8.19 s** |

Against the same check on the push-back-only binary: the abandoned turn goes
**132.32 s → 13.18 s** and the request waiting behind it **126.35 s → 7.13 s**.
Round 2's `reused 516` is the resumable prefill firing live and unasked: the
second curl resumed from where round 1's cancel stopped. Every cancelled request
is now in `owui_report.sh`, marked `cancelled`.

### Instrument findings (three, all caught by the phase check)

- **A decode-phase cancel timed from SUBMIT is a guess about two clocks.** The
  first attempt asked for 20 s on a 27-token prompt whose whole turn lasted
  12.3 s: the cancel never fired, `cancelled=False error=None`, the step
  measured nothing. `--cancel-phase decode` now asks for a long answer and
  counts from the FIRST TOKEN, so the phase is by construction.
- **`--cancel-phase prefill` is unreachable over `--url`.** `urlopen` returns
  when the response headers arrive, and the gateway commits the SSE stream on
  the engine's first DATA frame (glm53 sends no ACCEPT), so the harness cannot
  see the request until a token exists: over HTTP `--cancel N` has always meant
  "hang up N s after the first token". It is refused now, with the reason. The
  live prefill-phase case is the `curl -m 8`, a client that stops reading before
  any token — which is what a browser tab closing actually is.
- **`--cancel` used to print its verdict and leave the exit code at 0**, so a
  chain could serve an engine that ignores CANCEL and call it gated. It is a
  verdict now, and the chain's live step reverts on its own bound (30 s) and on
  the absence of a `CANCEL … at prefill` line.

Regression test `c/tests/test_glm53_cancel_frames.c`: includes `glm53.c` with
`GLM53_NO_MAIN` and drives the real stdin (a pipe, unbuffered, the same
`select()`) through eight cases — the CANCEL behind a queued SUBMIT, the queued
request arriving intact and in order, NOT_FOUND for a foreign id, STOP dropped,
an empty pipe that must not block or invent a cancel, IMAGE pushed back with its
CANCEL still readable after it, two queued SUBMITs then the cancel, and EOF as a
cancel. It has teeth: with the queue branch disabled it fails at
`serve_cancel_pending(&c) == 1`. No model, no Vulkan, seconds, and it runs in the
chain before the gate. `test_openai_server`: 170 tests OK (169 before).

### The acceptance gate on the user's path (2026-09-09 10:12)

`tools/hot-expert/accept_live.sh` against the served P7b binary
(f68d1cac…), Open WebUI 0.11.3 with tools and memory on, 53 s end to end:

| check | result |
|---|---|
| 1. UI-shaped new chat A (saved chat + session + memory) | 4 430 tokens, reused 4 419, ttft 2.24 s |
| 2. UI-shaped new chat B, other question — must be warm | **PASS** 4 432 tokens, reused 4 419, ttft 2.62 s |
| 3. API follow-up turn with memory on (the pin) | **PASS** 147 tokens, reused 136, 2.3 s |
| 4. a short request behind an abandoned 1 321-token one (CANCEL) | **PASS** answered in 8.1 s |

Note the 2.6 s on a new chat, not the 21 s of the P7b table: with one
memory the block is byte-stable, so P7b's after-restore plan captured the
prefix up to the question itself (`CKPT hit prefix=4419`), and a new chat
prefills only its question. `serve_candidate.sh` makes this gate the last
step of every chain; `accept_live.sh --canary` runs daily at 05:00 UTC.

### Driving the real client: `accept_ui.sh` (2026-09-09 10:55–12:57)

The acceptance gate above still *imitated* the browser. This one is the
browser: Playwright's Chromium on the owner's Mac at the rig's IPv4, a new
chat, a typed question, `Enter`, and the first token measured when it appears
in the assistant bubble. Same turn, three vantage points:

| vantage | first token | note |
|---|---:|---|
| engine (`[req] ttft`) | 2.23–2.61 s | what every gate on this track measured |
| browser, Playwright Chromium | **3.58 / 3.82 s** | what the person waits for |
| browser, system Google Chrome (fallback) | 13.64 / 15.88 s | headless-Chrome overhead, not the service |

Three traps, all found by running it rather than reasoning about it: the
first draft timed the *user's own* bubble (0.05 s — the same error as the
gates it replaces, measuring the wrong thing); `rome.local` resolves through
mDNS to a link-local IPv6 that Chrome refuses (`ERR_NAME_NOT_RESOLVED`) while
curl copes, so the gate resolves the rig's IPv4 first; and an unpinned
`playwright-core` installs a driver whose browser build is not in the cache,
silently falling back to system Chrome and inflating every number by ~10 s —
`PW_VERSION` is pinned to the cached build and the RESULT line carries
`browser=`.

## Response-time matrix on Open WebUI (2026-09-09 12:20–12:50)

Driven in a real browser (`tools/hot-expert/ui/ui_matrix.sh`, Playwright Chromium on the
owner's Mac at the rig's IPv4), tools and memory ON — the configuration the owner uses.
Every first turn therefore carries the same fixed **4 419-token prefix** (24 builtin tools +
the pinned memory block), which P7's checkpoint serves for free; context grows the way a
person grows it, by pasting text into a new chat. Each size uses *different* filler: the
first run reused the same passage truncated, and the checkpoint from a shorter row fired on
a longer one (reused 4 781 and 5 141 against the 4 419 prefix), which flattered every large
row. Browser numbers are what the person waits for; engine numbers are the `[req]` line.

| pasted | turn | engine prompt | reused | new | engine ttft | browser first token | browser total | gen | ms/new token |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 1 | 4428 | 4419 | 9 | 1.81 s | 2.81 s | 3.63 s | 2 | 201 |
| 0 | 2 | 4444 | 4430 | 14 | 2.74 s | 3.79 s | 3.80 s | 2 | 196 |
| 500 | 1 | 4672 | 4419 | 253 | 42.04 s | 48.88 s | 49.71 s | 17 | 166 |
| 500 | 2 | 4688 | 4419 | 269 | 45.53 s | 46.85 s | 46.86 s | 3 | 169 |
| 1000 | 1 | 4918 | 4419 | 499 | 86.38 s | 87.38 s | 88.19 s | 2 | 173 |
| 1000 | 2 | 4934 | 4920 | 14 | 2.72 s | 4.04 s | 4.85 s | 3 | 194 |
| 2000 | 1 | 5434 | 4419 | 1015 | 180.96 s | 188.01 s | 188.83 s | 18 | 178 |
| 2000 | 2 | 5450 | 4419 | 1031 | 185.04 s | 187.06 s | 187.07 s | 4 | 179 |
| 3500 | 1 | 6142 | 4419 | 1723 | 317.99 s | 324.19 s | 325.01 s | 14 | 185 |
| 3500 | 2 | 6158 | 4419 | 1739 | 321.33 s | 323.44 s | 323.45 s | 4 | 185 |

**What it says.** A first turn costs its own new tokens at 166–185 ms each, rising with
context (attention grows); the 4 419-token prefix costs nothing. A follow-up turn is flat at
~2.8 s in the engine and ~4 s in the browser *when the prefix holds* — independent of how
much context sits behind it. The browser adds a consistent ~1 s over the engine's own ttft,
which no gate on this track had ever measured.

**And the finding the matrix produced, which matters more than the timings.** Three of the
five follow-up turns did **not** reuse (rows 500, 2000, 3500: `REUSE 4419` and a full
re-prefill, 45–321 s). The arithmetic names the cause: those turns generated 17, 18 and 14
tokens while the next prompt grew by only 16 — the follow-up question alone is ~14 — so
roughly 15 generated tokens vanished. They are the model's `<think>` block: Open WebUI
stores an assistant turn as `content` only (verified in `webui.db`: keys `content`, `done`,
`model`), so reasoning the engine generated is dropped from the next request, the rendered
prompt stops matching the tokens the session holds, and the conversation re-prefills from
the checkpoint. Replies with no reasoning (gen 2, 6, 50 — measured) reuse exactly
`prompt + gen`. This is a consequence of the upstream `dev` merge (#1327/#1278): the glm53
renderer now always opens `<|assistant|><think>`, so the model emits reasoning far more
often than the fork's pre-merge prompt did.

**Proposed fix — P8, the reply pin.** The gateway already pins the memory block per
conversation (`COLI_PREFIX_PIN`). The same mechanism fits here: remember the exact text the
engine generated for the last assistant turn, and when a later request carries that turn's
visible content, render the full generated text in its place, so the prompt reproduces the
tokens the session holds. Alternative, smaller and cruder: close the reasoning block for
glm53 when the client did not ask for thinking (the fork's pre-merge behaviour), which
removes the reasoning rather than preserving it. The gate is this matrix: every follow-up
row must reuse `prompt + gen`.

## P8 (2026-09-09 15:15–16:05) — the reply pin: built, reproduced, **UNGATED** (the Mac left the rig's network mid-chain)

**Status: not in service, and the rig is back exactly where it started.** The
gate's offline half exited 3 on a self-comparison (below) and `p8_chain.sh`
reverted, **verified**, at 16:05:15: binary `f68d1cac…`, `kda_step.spv`
`b7d57f05…`, tree back at `356cdd3`, `gateway: 1 engine: 1`, `/v1/models` 200
(the restart loop broke on it — stop, reset, copy and restart took 99 s from the
verdict line). The Mac then left the rig's network and the link did not come
back within the session, so the LIVE half was never run. The code, the harness
and the gate are committed on `perf/p8-reply-pin` and were pushed to the rig as
`p0-sync` before the link dropped; what is missing is measurement, not work.

### The bug, confirmed on the served gateway before anything changed

The browser matrix (previous section) said three follow-up turns of five did not
reuse. The mechanism is now measured directly, through Open WebUI's own backend
(`tools/hot-expert/p8_reasoning_case.sh`: saved chat + `session_id` + `chat_id` +
memory on, the assistant turn read back **out of `webui.db`**, which is what its
front end sends on the next turn):

| turn-1 question (after N filler tokens) | N | prompt 1 | gen 1 | ttft 1 | stored `content` | prompt 2 | **REUSE** | ttft 2 | expected REUSE |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| "Reply with the single word OK." (**the matrix's own 500-token filler, byte for byte**) | 500 | 4672 | 2 | 42.74 s | 2 chars | 4688 | 4674 | 2.69 s | 4674 ✅ |
| "Reply with the single word OK." (different filler) | 500 | 4680 | 2 | 43.51 s | 2 chars | 4696 | 4682 | 2.72 s | 4682 ✅ |
| "…what is 17 times 23, plus 6?" | 0 | 4443 | 3 | 4.37 s | 3 chars | 4460 | 4446 | 2.70 s | 4446 ✅ |
| "Think step by step, then give only the final number…" | 0 | 4448 | 20 | 5.21 s | 33 chars | 4482 | 4468 | 2.77 s | 4468 ✅ |
| **"There are 3 boxes… How many are in box B? Give only the number."** | 500 | **4718** | **29** | 50.52 s | **6 chars** | 4736 | **4419** | **54.07 s** | 4747 ❌ |

The last row **is** the item: 29 generated tokens, 6 characters of visible
answer, and the follow-up falls all the way back to the 4 419-token checkpoint
and pays 54.07 s where the same conversation shape costs 2.7 s. The engine's own
lines show why it cannot do better: the KDA/DSA state cannot rewind past
prompt + reply, so a prompt that diverges from the slot at position 4 718 is not
a shorter prefix of it — it is a different sequence, and the slot is unusable.

**Two instrument findings, both of which change how this case must be written.**

1. **The matrix's own prompt does not reproduce the bug on demand.** Its
   500-token filler plus "Reply with the single word OK." — reproduced here byte
   for byte, `prompt 1 = 4672`, the matrix's own number — answers with `gen=2`
   and an **empty** `<think>` block at temperature 0 *and* at 0.8. The browser
   rows that reasoned did so by sampling, three times in five. A turn with no
   reasoning reuses its prefix perfectly with or without a pin, so a fixed
   prompt of that shape is not a regression test. Direct probe against the
   served gateway, both temperatures, identical results:

   | question | gen | reasoning | visible |
   |---|---:|---:|---:|
   | "Reply with the single word OK." | 2 | 0 B | 2 B |
   | bat-and-ball | 5 | 0 B | 5 B |
   | "Think step by step … fifth letter" | 4 | 2 B | 1 B |
   | **three boxes, 45 balls** | **29** | **34 B** | **6 B** |

   The three-box system of equations reasons every time; that is why the gate
   uses it.
2. **Open WebUI stores the assistant turn with keys `content`, `done`, `model`,
   `output`, `usage`, `id`, `role`, `timestamp`, `parentId`, `childrenIds` — and
   `content` is the visible text only.** Read straight out of `webui.db` by the
   harness, so the gate no longer takes the record's word for it.

### The fix

`c/openai_server.py` only; `c/glm53.c` untouched and the built binary
byte-identical (`f68d1cac…`, checked by sha in the chain). Knob
`COLI_REPLY_PIN`, default 1, 0 restores the pre-P8 behaviour. The gateway keeps
the RAW generated text of each assistant turn — before the reasoning splitter,
before any strip — keyed by conversation (the key `conversation_cache_slot` uses,
minus the per-turn context block so it survives Open WebUI's memory re-ranking),
LRU 64 conversations × 8 turns, and renders it back **verbatim** after the
`<think>` the generation prompt opened when a later request carries that turn's
visible content. Not reassembled from `(reasoning, content)`: `content.strip()`
and the `<think>{reasoning}</think>{content}` rebuild are exactly what breaks the
match. A reply truncated inside `<think>` has no visible part at all, so each
remembered generation carries its assistant-turn **index** and an empty stored
content is restored only against an empty remembered one at the same index in
the same conversation. `[reply-pin] key=<8 hex> turn=<n> restored=<chars>` under
`COLI_REQ_LOG`.

### What the gate did measure

| step | result |
|---|---|
| build | binary **unchanged by the build** (`f68d1cac…`), 9 shaders unchanged — P8 is gateway-only, as claimed |
| C tests | `test_qwen38_prefix/config/metrics/serve_framing`, `test_serve_poll`, `test_glm53_cancel_frames` — all exit 0 |
| gateway python tests | passed on the rig (the chain proceeds to the gate only on exit 0); on the Mac: **16 new** (`c/tests/test_glm53_reply_pin.py`) **+ 207 existing** (`test_openai_server`, `test_anthropic_messages`, `test_chat_thinking`, `test_openai_tools_e2e`) |
| step 1 (a) teacher forcing | **IDENTICAL, 782 positions** |
| step 1 (b) last-position logits | **cosine=1.0000000 max_abs=0**, argmax 7664 vs 7664 |
| step 1 (c) serve-path TTFT | 27 tok 2.84/2.72 s vs 2.92/2.82 s → **0.97×**; 390 tok 40.84/40.65 vs 40.74/40.59 → **1.00×**; 1 236 tok 139.35/139.39 vs 139.46/139.51 → **1.00×**; decode 5.6/5.8, 5.9/6.1, 4.9/4.7 tok/s |
| step 2 tworeq | **IDENTICAL across 4 requests at `COLI_KDA_GPU=2` and at `=0`, 0 forcing lines** (rc 0) |
| steps 3, 4, accept_live, accept_ui, ui_matrix | **not run** |

**The step-1 failure is the harness, and it is worth its own line in this file.**
Step 1 exited 3 on `speedup gate: FAIL` — at the 27-token row, 0.97× — with the
pristine and the candidate being **the same file**, sha256 equal, as the chain
had just printed. A binary cannot be slower than itself; what the row measured
is the noise floor of a 2.7-second measurement across two fresh engine
processes, the same artefact §P5b recorded as an allocator lottery (5.8 ms/token
in `mla.attn` on identical source) and the same row that made P5b's first run
exit 3 at `MIN_SPEEDUP=1.0`. `p8_gate.sh` now compares the two binaries by
sha256 and, when they are identical, prints the table and downgrades the *speed*
verdict to informational while the oracle half stays fatal. That is a
categorical statement, not a threshold move: a candidate whose binary differs is
still judged at `MIN_SPEEDUP`.

**A second instrument defect, found in the gate's own verdict.** That run printed

```
  step 1  prefill_gate (ckpt off)      rc=3
  step 2  tworeq 4 slots, both knobs   rc=0
  step 3  reasoning case, on vs off    rc=0
  step 4  text identity                rc=0
```

under `GATE_STEPS=12` — steps 3 and 4 **never started**, and their initialised
`rc=0` reads exactly like a pass. It is the same class as this track's rule
"a live step that only prints a number is not a gate". `p8_gate.sh` now tracks
whether each step ran, prints `-- (not run)` when it did not, and says
`NOTE: this run did NOT gate the item` whenever the live half was skipped.

### What is left

The live half, in order, on the rig: `GATE_STEPS=34 p8_gate.sh` (the reasoning
case three times with the pin on, three times with it off as the control, then
text identity across the knob), `accept_live.sh`, and from a Mac **on the rig's
LAN** `accept_ui.sh` and
`ui/ui_matrix.sh --sizes 0,500,2000 --out ~/bench/matrix_p8.tsv`, whose turn-2
rows are the item's headline. `~/bench/p8_chain.sh` runs all of the rig-side
part unattended and restores the pristine on every failure path.

### P8 gated and in service (2026-09-09 21:24–22:10)

Three chain runs, and the first two failed on the harness rather than on P8 —
recorded because both were the same disease as P7b: a step that assumes the
previous one finished.

| run | verdict | cause |
|---|---|---|
| 20:33 | step 1 rc=1 | `prefill_profile.sh` demanded all 62 shards resident, which is unreachable once an engine has run; both oracle runs aborted and the gate compared two EMPTY files, printing `IDENTICAL (0 positions)` as a pass. Fixed in `9e2a90c`: an empty comparison is a FAIL, and `PROFILE_MIN_RESIDENT` is a knob with a second warm. |
| 21:10 | step 1 rc=1 | the pristine oracle produced its 782 positions, then the candidate's run hit `REFUSED: a glm53 is running` — the pristine's engine was still unmapping 180 GiB. Both TTFT halves refused for the same reason. `CLAUDE.md` states the rule and `p7_gate.sh` honours it; the shared gate did not. Fixed in `fec5ea2`: `wait_no_engine()` after each oracle side and around each `ttft_serve` run. |
| 21:24 | **rc=0, in service** | below |

**The gate that counted.** Step 1 `prefill_gate.sh` with checkpoints off: rc=0
(P8 is gateway-only; the binary is byte-identical to the pristine, `f68d1cac…`).
Step 2 `tworeq` at 4 slots: IDENTICAL at `COLI_KDA_GPU=2` and `=0`.

**Step 3, the controlled experiment** — a word problem that makes the model
reason, driven through Open WebUI's own backend, the assistant turn read back
out of `webui.db`:

| seed | p1 | gen1 | stored | p2 | REUSE2 | ttft2 | verdict |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| 1 pin ON | 4 718 | 29 | 6 ch | 4 761 | **4 747** = p1+gen1 | **2.77 s** | PASS |
| 2 pin ON | 4 708 | 27 | 2 ch | 4 749 | **4 735** | **2.81 s** | PASS |
| 3 pin ON | 4 713 | 27 | 2 ch | 4 754 | **4 740** | **2.77 s** | PASS |
| 1 pin OFF | 4 718 | 29 | 6 ch | 4 736 | 4 718 | 3.51 s | FAIL |
| 2 pin OFF | 4 708 | 27 | 2 ch | 4 724 | **4 419** | **52.22 s** | FAIL |
| 3 pin OFF | 4 713 | 27 | 2 ch | 4 729 | **4 419** | **53.28 s** | FAIL |

3/3 against 0/3: with the pin the follow-up reuses exactly `prompt + gen` and
answers in under three seconds; without it the conversation falls back to the
4 419-token checkpoint and pays ~52 s. Step 4 (text identity) rc=0 — the reply
the client receives is unchanged; the pin alters only what is sent back to the
engine.

**Browser gate** (`accept_ui.sh`, Playwright Chromium on the Mac): new chat A
first token **3.82 s**, new chat B **3.57 s**, both replies correct.

**One flake found, and it is not P8.** `accept_live.sh` check 4 (a short
request behind an abandoned one) failed once at 158.8 s, then reproduced 1 of 3
in isolation (255.5 s / 13.0 s / 7.8 s). In every failing run the engine log
carries **no `CANCEL` line at all**: the gateway never sent one, so the
abandoned request ran its whole prefill. The same service passed the same check
minutes earlier, so this is the pre-existing disconnect-detection race on
*non-streaming* requests (Open WebUI streams, so the owner's own path is the
one that works). Next item.

**Three harness defects this run exposed, all in the gates themselves.**
1. `accept_ui.sh` read `grep`'s exit status through a pipe, so a **failing** `accept_live`
   printed PASS. `PIPESTATUS[0]` now decides.
2. `accept_live.sh`'s checks reused a fixed question, so a second run of the same check
   sent an identical turn-2 — which the engine cannot reuse by design (`kv_prefix_reuse`
   needs at least one new token) and which read as a regression. Each run now carries a
   nonce.
3. Check 4 measured whatever the engine was already doing: a 160 MB checkpoint store plus a
   queue made a cancel check read 300 s. It now waits for an idle engine first.

## P9 (2026-09-10 00:19–01:44) — the conversation ledger: the gateway owns the rendering, and the engine's agreement is now a log line

Spec `P9-CONVERSATION-LEDGER-SPEC-2026-09.md`. Gateway: `c/openai_server.py`
gains the ledger and its match/decide state machine behind `COLI_LEDGER`
(default 1; 0 = today's path, P7's pin and P8's reply pin included). Engine:
`c/glm53.c` gains ONE trailing field on the `DONE … STAT` line — `reused` —
append-only, and `Engine._stats` reads it positionally and yields `None` on an
engine that does not send it. Knobs `COLI_LEDGER` (1), `COLI_LEDGER_MAX_CONV`
(64), `COLI_LEDGER_STRICT` (0).

Chain `~/bench/p9_chain.sh`, gate `p9_gate.sh p909100019`, pristine
`~/bench/glm53.p8` (**f68d1cac…**, the P8/P7b binary in service, snapshotted and
verified by the chain before anything stopped), candidate **34357e49…**,
all four steps rc=0, `accept_live.sh` rc=0, **in service since 2026-09-10
01:44**.

**Why this exists, from this file.** Prefix reuse rests on a contract nobody
enforced: the transcript the client re-sends must re-render to exactly the token
sequence the engine already holds. Open WebUI broke it three times in three days
(§P7 turn 2 reused 0/195 at 21.6 s; §P7b every new chat 362.94 s; §P8 a
follow-up at 54.07 s against 2.7 s), and every one of them was invisible except
as latency. The ledger keeps, per conversation, the exact pieces **the renderer
produced** for the prompt the engine ground, plus the raw text it generated, and
renders every later turn from that record; the client's copy is used only to
IDENTIFY each turn. Before submitting a continuation it asserts
`new_prompt.startswith(ledger.prompt + ledger.generated)`.

### Step 1 — `prefill_gate.sh`, `GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT=0`, `MIN_SPEEDUP=0.97`, private `COLI_CKPT_DIR`

The engine changes by one printf field, and the oracle says exactly that:
teacher forcing **IDENTICAL at 782 positions**, last-position logits
`cosine=1.0000000` **`max_abs=0`** (bit-identical, not merely close), argmax
7664 both.

| size | pristine ttft (both runs) | candidate ttft (both runs) | speedup | decode p/c |
|---:|---|---|---:|---|
| 30 | 2.9 s / 2.8 s | 2.9 s / 2.8 s | 0.99× | 5.8 / 5.9 |
| 300 | 40.8 s / 40.5 s | 40.7 s / 40.5 s | 1.00× | 5.9 / 6.0 |
| 1000 | 139.0 s / 139.2 s | 139.1 s / 139.2 s | 1.00× | 4.8 / 4.9 |

### Step 2 — `tworeq.py TWOREQ_SLOTS=4`

**IDENTICAL across 4 requests at `COLI_KDA_GPU=2` and at `=0`, zero
`forcing COLI_KDA_GPU=0` lines.**

### Step 3 — the three client behaviours, in THREE arms

The spec asks for "PASS with `COLI_LEDGER=1` and FAIL with `COLI_LEDGER=0`.
Taken literally that is not measurable for behaviours (a) and (b): at
`COLI_LEDGER=0` the gateway still has P7's pin and P8's reply pin, which were
built for exactly those two, so the control would pass and prove nothing about
either mechanism. So three arms — `on` (the item), `off` (today's path, pins
included: is the ledger a regression?), `bare` (`COLI_PREFIX_PIN=0
COLI_REPLY_PIN=0`: does the ledger subsume the class?). Three seeds each.
The expectation was written into `p9_gate.sh` before the run; the table is what
came out.

| behaviour | ledger ON | ledger OFF (today) | no mechanism |
|---|---|---|---|
| (a) re-ranked memory block | **3/3 PASS** | 3/3 PASS | **0/3 PASS** |
| (b) a reasoning reply | **3/3 PASS** | 3/3 PASS | **0/3 PASS** |
| (c) a client that trims whitespace | **3/3 PASS** | **0/3 PASS** | 0/3 PASS |

PASS = turn 2 REUSEs `prompt_tokens(turn 1) + gen(turn 1)`, ±2.

**(a) the re-ranked memory block** (`p9_memory_case.sh`: four temporary `context`
memories through the API, two turns through Open WebUI's own backend with
`features.memory` on, memories deleted on every exit path):

| arm | seed | p1 | gen1 | p2 | reused2 | expect | ttft2 | verdict |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| ON | 1 / 2 / 3 | 192 | 48 | 267 | **239 / 239 / 239** | 240 | **3.40 / 3.35 / 3.41 s** | PASS |
| OFF | 1 / 2 / 3 | 194 | 48 | 270 | 241 / 241 / 241 | 242 | 3.49 / 3.48 / 3.54 s | PASS |
| BARE | 1 / 2 / 3 | 193 | 48 | 259–261 | **0 / 180 / 179** | 241 | **26.33 / 9.44 / 9.28 s** | FAIL |

**(b) a reasoning reply** (`p8_reasoning_case.sh` unchanged — the three-box word
problem, which reasons at temperature 0 and 0.8, driven through Open WebUI's own
backend with the 24-tool block):

| arm | seed | p1 | gen1 | ttft1 | p2 | reused2 | expect | ttft2 | verdict |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| ON | 1 | 4 717 | 29 | 700.58 s | 4 760 | **4 746** | 4 746 | **2.75 s** | PASS |
| ON | 2 | 4 707 | 29 | 66.23 s | 4 750 | **4 736** | 4 736 | **2.80 s** | PASS |
| ON | 3 | 4 712 | 29 | 49.40 s | 4 755 | **4 741** | 4 741 | **2.75 s** | PASS |
| OFF | 1 / 2 / 3 | 4 718 / 4 708 / 4 713 | 29 / 27 / 27 | 70.28 / 67.02 / 49.13 s | | 4 747 / 4 735 / 4 740 | = | 2.81 / 2.76 / 2.77 s | PASS |
| BARE | 1 / 2 / 3 | 4 717 / 4 707 / 4 712 | 29 | 697.90 / 67.01 / 49.64 s | | **4 307 / 4 418 / 4 418** | 4 746 / 4 736 / 4 741 | **73.39 / 52.43 / 53.17 s** | FAIL |

**(c) a client that trims whitespace** (`p9_strip_case.sh`, synthetic, straight
at the gateway: a first user message ending in whitespace, handed back trimmed
together with the reply — and with `reasoning_content` still attached, so P8's
pin is not what is being measured):

| arm | seed | p1 | gen1 | p2 | reused2 | expect | ttft2 | verdict |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| ON | 1 / 2 / 3 | 39 / 38 / 39 | 7 / 8 / 7 | 70 / 69 / 70 | **46 / 46 / 46** | 46 | 2.92 / 2.84 / 2.90 s | PASS |
| OFF | 1 / 2 / 3 | 39 | 7 / 8 / 8 | 69 / 70 / 70 | **0 / 0 / 0** | 46 / 47 / 47 | 6.80 / 6.87 / 6.73 s | FAIL |
| BARE | 1 / 2 / 3 | 39 | 12 / 8 / 7 | 74 / 70 / 69 | **0 / 0 / 0** | 51 / 47 / 46 | 7.13 / 6.80 / 6.72 s | FAIL |

**Row (c) is the item's own claim.** P8's reply pin already tolerates a trimmed
REPLY; nothing before P9 tolerated a trimmed QUESTION, because the user turn is
rendered from the client's bytes and one trailing space moves the prompt at that
position. Today's gateway reuses **0 of 46** there — and, measured in the same
run, routes turn 2 to a *different KV slot*, because `conversation_cache_slot`
hashes the first user message and the trimmed one hashes elsewhere. The ledger
fixes both: the turn is identified on normalised text and the conversation owns
its slot.

### Step 4 — divergence correctness: slow is allowed, wrong is not

`p9_divergence.sh` builds three two-turn conversations, sends the diverged
transcript against the warm ledger, the gate **restarts the gateway** so the cold
phase meets an empty one, and the two texts are compared byte for byte.

| case | ledger's own line | warm | cold | verdict |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|
| regenerate | `ledger=reset reason=shorter at=4 kept=3` | 8 B | 8 B | **IDENTICAL** |
| edit an earlier message | `ledger=reset reason=diverged at=3` | 5 B | 5 B | **IDENTICAL** |
| branch | `ledger=reset reason=diverged at=3` | 4 B | 4 B | **IDENTICAL** |

That property is why the ledger replays **nothing** on a `shorter` or `diverged`
turn, which is a deliberate departure from spec §3.2 ("truncate the ledger to the
matched turns and render from there"). Replaying the matched turns buys no reuse
— the slot holds MORE tokens than the shorter prompt, so `cached < total` is
false and `serve_one` re-prefills whichever bytes it is given — and it would cost
exactly the byte-identity above. The record is rebuilt from the diverged request
on DONE, so the next turn continues from what the engine actually holds.

### `accept_live.sh` on the served candidate, with its new check 2b

| check | result |
|---|---|
| 1. UI new chat A | prompt 4 442, reused **4 418**, ttft **4.79 s** |
| 2. UI new chat B (warm) | PASS prompt 4 444, reused 4 418, ttft **5.12 s** |
| 2b. ledger invariant | PASS 0 MISMATCH, 0 `ledger=broken` |
| 3. API follow-up (continuation) | PASS prompt 169, reused 146, total 3.4 s |
| 4. request behind an abandoned one | PASS 7.1 s |

### The arithmetic that makes `expect_reuse` honest, and the measurement that fixed it

`slot_remember(slot, sequence, total)` leaves prompt + generated in the slot, and
`session->filled` reaches the same number because every generated token but the
last is fed back — **and the last one is too, except when the turn stopped on its
token budget**, where the loop breaks before `forward_span` and the session is
one position short (`glm53.c serve_one`). So

    expect_reuse = prompt_tokens(prev) + completion_tokens(prev) − (1 if length-limited)

That −1 is not a guess: measured 2026-09-10 00:17, turn 1 prompt 4 543, gen 96,
`length` → **reused 4 638 = 4543 + 96 − 1**, and the same −1 on every memory-case
row above (gen hit the 48-token budget). Six earlier confirmations of the +0 form
are already in this file (§P8's three rows, and the browser matrix's 4428+2,
4672+2, 5434+2). Across the whole gate every continuation printed
`expect_reuse == engine_reuse`.

### Four things the run showed that were not in the plan

1. **P7's pin was also NORMALISING the system message.** `pin_context_blocks`
   rewrites the leading system content as `base.strip() + "\n\n" + block`. With
   the ledger on, that pin is off and Open WebUI's own bytes go through — which
   differ, so the 4 419-token checkpoint captured under the pin did not fire and
   the first UI-shaped request paid a full **700.58 s** prefill once. It then
   captured **4 307** (the boundary *before* the memory block, which
   `glm53_prefix_cut` hints), and every later UI request hit it: the same case
   cost 66.23 s and 49.40 s on the next two seeds. Post-restart the served
   checkpoints read `4 712 / 4 418` — one token shorter than P7's `4 713 / 4 419`,
   which is that same normalisation, measured. The new boundary is the better one:
   it does not depend on the memory block at all.
2. **A 200-token conversation's checkpoint evicts the tool block's.** The memory
   case stores 179/180/181/193-token checkpoints (162 MB each) and the cache holds
   four, so the 4 307 checkpoint was evicted between arms and the first reasoning
   run of the next arm paid 697.90 s again. This is exactly the churn §P7 and §P7b
   flagged (`GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT_MIN=128` lets conversation-local prefixes in); it
   cost this gate ~24 minutes and is recorded rather than tuned away.
3. **The first draft of the memory case was 11 minutes per run.** A UI-shaped
   turn 1 whose `<memory_context>` has changed matches no checkpoint: measured
   682.59 s, plus a 302 MB store that evicted a useful one. The case now uses the
   API shape §P7 itself used (turn 1 = 192 tokens), runs in ~35 s, and fixes the
   memories' text so the gate stops churning the engine's cache.
4. **`ledger=reset reason=tools` fires on the UI path** whenever a request's tool
   list differs from the one its conversation started with. The signature is a
   GUARD here, not a pin — spec §2 would keep the old tool block, and that is the
   one place this build deliberately refuses it: rendering a declaration the
   client no longer sends would let the model call a tool the client cannot
   execute, and the answer the user reads would change. The safety rule outranks
   the convenience, and a changed tool list re-prefills exactly as it does today.

### Tests

`c/tests/test_glm53_ledger.py`, 43 new: 38 on the state machine at its own level
(exact continuation, client-shorter, diverged mid-history, unknown key, two
conversations colliding on the derived key, a trimmed reply, a trimmed question,
a reply truncated inside `<think>`, the broken invariant, `COLI_LEDGER_STRICT`,
slot ownership, the `expect_reuse` arithmetic, and the safety rule asserted
directly — one rendered piece per message the client sent, never one more) and 5
driving the real HTTP path against a glm53-shaped fake engine. `267 tests` pass
on the rig (43 ledger + 17 reply-pin + 207 existing), and the glm53 renderer is
still **byte-identical to the checkpoint's own `chat_template.jinja`** on all 25
cases after gaining its `parts` hook.

### Steps 5 and 6 are still owed, and why

`p9_ui_multiturn.sh` (a ten-turn browser conversation, `ui_probe.mjs
--follow-ups N`), `accept_ui.sh` and `ui_matrix.sh --sizes 0,500,2000` had not
produced a clean transcript when this section was written. Both attempts ran
into a **second session driving the rig at the same time** — `cancel2_chain.sh`,
which restarts the gateway and runs `accept_live`-shaped checks every ~3 minutes
(visible in `~/glm53_server.log` at 01:52, 01:55 and 01:58 as the same
`1 321`-token abandoned request, the same 15-token follow-up, and the same
`key=92b0eb68 ledger=reset reason=shorter` pair). A browser turn queued behind
that measures the other session's queue, so no number from those runs is
recorded here. **One benchmark at a time is a rule this file already states; two
sessions is the case it did not cover.** The gate's own evidence for the
invariant stands on its own: every continuation in steps 3 and 4 and in
`accept_live` printed `expect_reuse == engine_reuse`, with zero `MISMATCH` and
zero `ledger=broken` across the whole chain.

Two harness defects in this item's own files were found by that second session
and are fixed in the tree, both of the "a gate that cannot fail" family this
track keeps recording:

- `p9_ui_multiturn.sh` matched `[ledger]` with `l.startswith("[ledger] ")`, but
  `~/start_glm53.sh` timestamps every line through an `awk` pipe, so it matched
  **nothing** and could only ever report `lines=0`. It failed closed this time;
  a real MISMATCH would have been just as invisible.
- `accept_live.sh`'s new check 2b printed **PASS with 0 checked** after checks
  1–2, which drive two NEW chats and therefore no continuation at all. The call
  after check 3 now requires at least one checked continuation.

### The night's real headline: five places, one lesson (2026-09-09/10)

P8 and P9 both landed, but the engine work was never what cost the time. Five
separate defects showed up in the *orchestration*, and they are the same
defect wearing different clothes — **a step that assumes the previous one
finished, or a check that can pass while seeing nothing**:

| where | what it did | fix |
|---|---|---|
| `prefill_gate.sh` | both oracle runs aborted on a 100 % residency floor (unreachable minutes after the gateway's engine is killed), then it compared two **empty** files and printed `teacher_forcing: IDENTICAL (0 positions)` | refuse a verdict under 100 compared positions; `PROFILE_MIN_RESIDENT` is a knob, 97 for an oracle half |
| `accept_ui.sh` | read `grep`'s status through a pipe, so a **failing** `accept_live` reported PASS | `PIPESTATUS[0]` |
| `accept_live.sh` | fixed question ⇒ a second run sent an identical turn 2, unreusable by design, read as a regression; and `2b. ledger invariant` printed **PASS with 0 checked** | nonce per run; `ledger_check … strict` requires ≥ 1 continuation |
| `p9_ui_multiturn.sh` | matched `[ledger]` at line start while `~/start_glm53.sh` timestamps every line — could only ever report zero | match the marker anywhere |
| the rig itself | two Claude sessions worked it at once; one restarted the gateway under the other's acceptance run, truncating the log and producing a MISMATCH that was pure collision | `rig_lock.sh` + `run_chain.sh` as the only entry point + a lock-aware `gateway_watchdog.sh` (cron */5) |

A sixth, of the same family, found by running the repo's own python suite on
the Mac with `PYTHONPATH=c`: **the Makefile's `glm53` rule never listed
`serve_poll.h`**, though `glm53.c` has included it since the CANCEL work, so
editing that header left the engine stale. `tests/test_makefile_deps.py` had
been failing on exactly that and no chain noticed, because chains build from a
clean checkout where every rule fires anyway (`4e7a6c9`).

What actually caught the two *real* regressions this week was neither a gate
nor a test: it was driving the owner's own client. P7b (a 380-second new chat)
and P8 (a 52-second follow-up) were both found that way. Hence the standing
rule — a gate must measure **the request after** the one it tests — and the
three instruments that now enforce it: `accept_live.sh` on the rig,
`accept_ui.sh` in a real browser, and the 05:00 canary.

## CANCEL retry (2026-09-10 02:27–02:32) — an abandoned request stops holding the engine

`accept_live.sh` check 4 had been flaky since it was written: a short request
behind an abandoned one answered in 7 s twice and then in 158 s, and in
isolation it reproduced **1 in 3** (255.5 / 13.0 / 7.8 s). In every failing run
the engine log carried **no `CANCEL` line at all**.

**The first fix was wrong and the gate caught it.** `740a9e2` deferred the
CANCEL until the engine's ACCEPT frame, on the theory that a cancel naming an
id the engine has not dequeued is answered `NOT_FOUND` and lost. ACCEPT only
arrives once prefill is done, so it killed the path that already worked
(`CANCEL 5 at prefill pos=128/1321`): 147.5 / 156.0 / 162.1 / 155.5 / 155.7 s,
**5/5 with no CANCEL**, against the 1-in-3 it was meant to cure. Reverted by
its own chain. colibri-77 then measured plain P9 at **7.0 / 7.0 / 6.8 s, +1
CANCEL each**, which cleared P9 of any part in it.

**What landed** (`3d80c07` + `07fa39a`, gateway python only, engine untouched):
send the first CANCEL immediately as before; treat an `ERROR NOT_FOUND` ack as
*too early* and re-send every 250 ms while the client stays gone, 20 attempts.
The second commit is what makes the first possible — the dispatcher popped a
request's routing entry on **every** error frame, so a `NOT_FOUND` stranded a
request that was still running and the retry had nowhere to deliver its ack.

**Gate** (`cancel3_chain.sh`, through `run_chain.sh` so the rig lock held —
its first production run): `check4.sh 6`, each cycle ≤ 45 s **and** a `CANCEL`
line, then `accept_live.sh` in full. Reconstructed from the engine's own log,
because the chain's stdout was mangled by a control character:

| cycle | abandoned request | cancelled at | next request |
|---:|---|---|---:|
| 1 | 1 325 tok | `pos=128/1325`, 12.86 s | **6.69 s** |
| 2 | 1 325 tok | `pos=128/1325`, 12.80 s | **6.65 s** |
| 3 | 1 325 tok | `pos=128/1325`, 12.83 s | **6.61 s** |
| 4 | 1 325 tok | `pos=256/1325`, 13.67 s | **7.47 s** |
| 5 | 1 325 tok | `pos=384/1325`, 14.51 s | **8.26 s** |
| 6 | 1 325 tok | `pos=512/1325`, 14.89 s | **8.69 s** |

6/6 cancelled during prefill, 6/6 under the bound, plus `accept_live` check 4
at 6.9 s and the ledger invariant clean (1 checked, 0 MISMATCH). In service:
tree `07fa39a`, binary `34357e49…` unchanged.

The creeping cancel position (128 → 512) is the resumable prefill from the
CANCEL item working as designed: each abandoned attempt leaves its completed
chunks in the slot, so the next one resumes further along.

**A hole in this gate, found by reading its own log and closed the same
night:** the pass criterion counted cycles *above* the bound, so a run that
produced no readable lines would have reported "0 bad" and passed on nothing —
the same shape as the empty-oracle pass and the 0-checked ledger alarm. It now
requires all six cycles to parse and treats unparseable output as failure.

### A short chat must not evict the tool block's checkpoint (2026-09-10 02:34–02:39)

The engine keeps 4 checkpoint slots and captures any prefix over
`GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT_MIN` (default 128), so a 170-token API chat stored ~160 MB
and evicted one of the ~4 400-token Open WebUI captures — measured inside the
P9 gate, which lost its 4 307 capture between arms and paid ~24 minutes for
it. The owner's own 05:00 canary makes exactly such a request, so this was
self-inflicted every morning.

**In service: `GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT_MIN=1024`** (`~/start_glm53.sh`, env only, no
rebuild). Verified with the setting live: a two-turn 170-token conversation
produced **`CKPT store` +0**, `accept_live` PASS on all four checks plus the
ledger invariant, all four disk slots still holding ≥ 4 300-token prefixes,
and `CKPT hit prefix=4418` on the next UI chat. The trade is explicit: a
500-token prefix now re-prefills in ~40 s rather than being cached, where
losing a 4 400-token one costs ~7 minutes.

**The proper fix is value-based eviction — keep the longest prefix, not the
most recent — and it belongs in the engine.** This is the safe half, done at
night; the LRU still evicts by recency among prefixes above the minimum.

**Seventh instance of the night's pattern, and mine.** The first attempt at
this chain died on `mark=$(grep -ac "CKPT store" "$LOG" || echo 0)`: when grep
finds nothing it prints `0` *and* exits 1, so the fallback appends a second
`0`, the arithmetic fails, and under `set -u` the chain aborted **after
patching the serving script and restarting the gateway but before verifying
anything** — leaving an unverified setting live. The same idiom parked a chain
for an hour earlier tonight (`p8_chain.sh`). `ckptmin_verify.sh` counts
without the fallback and tolerates grep's exit status.


## P10 (2026-09-10 06:50–08:45) — value-based checkpoint eviction: the cache stops preferring the capture nobody uses

### The before-picture, from the serving log rather than from theory

Four checkpoint slots, all full, one restart's history (02:36 → 06:40):

| slot | prefix | size | `CKPT hit` |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 4 707 | 315 MB | 0 |
| 1 | 4 307 | 302 MB | 0 |
| 2 | 4 712 | 315 MB | 0 |
| 3 | **4 418** | 305 MB | **9** |

Slot 3 is Open WebUI's tool block. The eviction rule was LRU inside each `kind`
pass (`ckpt_store`, `g_ckpt[i].used < g_ckpt[victim].used`), and after a disk
load all four clocks are set within one loop — so LRU cannot rank these four at
all, and the only capture that has ever paid for itself survives by the accident
of being the one that gets touched. Measured twice as a failure: a 170-token API
chat evicting a 4 307 capture inside the P9 gate (~24 min), and the 05:00 canary
reproducing it nightly. `GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT_MIN=1024` was the blunt half.

### The change

`PrefixCkpt` gains `unsigned hits`; the score is `len × hits`; a capture is born
at `hits = 1` (so its score is its length — the length-only rule as the opening
guess, and a fresh capture is never the automatic next victim); `ckpt_restore`
increments the winner only; `used` remains as tie-break, so the order stays
total. `GLM53_CKPT_VALUE_EVICT=0` restores the old LRU exactly.
Persistence is a trailing `uint32` written **under the unchanged `G53CKPT1`
magic** — an old file reads short and yields `hits = 1`, a new file is identical
to an old reader up to the end of the blob — updated in place on a hit (4 bytes
at `28 + len*4 + bytes`), because rewriting 305 MB per restore is not on the
table and a stale disk copy would hand back `hits = 1` after every restart.
Confirmed on disk: slot 3's file grew 305286436 → **305286440** bytes while the
other three stayed old-format, and the counter came back across a restart
(`CKPT hit prefix=4418 slot=3 hits=4` on the first restore after it).

### The flood (p10_gate.sh step 3), two arms, one seed

20 short chats then one UI-shaped new chat, both arms started from a
byte-identical copy of the live directory, `GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT_MIN=128`, the tool
block warmed to `hits = 4` in both:

| arm | first store evicted | 20 stores | UI chat after |
|---|---|---|---|
| **value (`=1`)** | `4307/1`, the lowest score | all in **slot 1**, each evicting the previous ~145-token capture | **reused 4 418/4 442, ttft 4.57 s** |
| LRU (`=0`) | `4707/1`, the oldest | slots 0,1,2,3 then round again | **reused 0/4 442, ttft 668.56 s** |

**146×.** The control's 4th store names its victim in the engine's own
accounting: `evicted=4418/4` — LRU discarded the only capture with any hits on
it. In the value arm the flood eats itself and never reaches a ≥ 4 300 capture.

### The hits term, isolated (p10_hits_case.sh)

The flood alone would pass for a *length-only* rule, since a 145-token capture
loses to a 4 418 one on length. So: two slots seeded {4 418, a longer stale
4 712}, same binary, same knob (`=1`), same trigger capture, and the only
difference is the counter.

| the tool block's counter | evicted | UI chat after |
|---|---|---|
| **hits = 9** (earned by 4 UI turns) | `4712/1`, the stale one | **4.98 s** |
| hits = 1 — the counter truncated off, i.e. a genuine pre-P10 file | **`4418/1`** | **667.82 s** |

### Oracles and neutrality

`teacher_forcing IDENTICAL (782 positions)`, `logits cosine=1.0000000 max_abs=0
argmax 7664 vs 7664`, serve-path TTFT **1.02× / 1.00× / 1.00×** at 27/390/1 236
tokens with decode unmoved — all with `GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT=0`, where none of the
new code executes. `tworeq` at `TWOREQ_SLOTS=4`: IDENTICAL at `COLI_KDA_GPU=2`
and `=0`, **0 forcing lines**. Six C tests green. Shaders unchanged.

### In service

Binary `0cbf48a54e2d0cc82a7a7531a685ee329a51afc3400e1a44d95351595b2c9387`.
`~/start_glm53.sh` changed in one line: **`GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT_MIN` 1024 → 128**,
the code default — the threshold no longer has to shield, and a 500-token prefix
is cacheable again. `accept_live` PASS (all four checks plus the ledger
invariant) twice; `accept_ui` PASS from the Mac with **first token 4.83 s and
4.32 s** in the bundled Chromium. `ui_matrix --sizes 0,500,2000`
(`ui/matrix-p10-2026-09-10.tsv`) turn-2 rows unchanged against P9's:
`engine_new = 14` on all three sizes, ttft 2.77 / 2.73 / 2.87 s against 2.74 /
2.74 / 2.89 s.

### One number worth keeping

A **145-token capture costs 161 MB**; the 4 418-token tool block costs 305 MB.
The blob is therefore ~**154 MB fixed plus ~34 KB per token** — the KDA
recurrence and convolution window do not scale with length, only the DSA rows
do. The capture LRU kept preferring returned **3.8 % of the tokens for 52 % of
the memory**. This is the quantitative reason the cache is worth ranking at all.

### Four defects, all in the gate, none in the engine

1. **Step 2 ran `tworeq` with none of the required environment** — no
   `COLI_VULKAN`, no `COLI_VK_DEV2/3`, no `COLI_VK_EXPERTS2/3=1695`, no shader
   path, no `OMP_*` pinning, and the binary passed as `argv` where `tworeq.py`
   reads `TWOREQ_EXE`. The KDA device pool held 0, the P6b guard printed
   `forcing COLI_KDA_GPU=0`, and the chain **failed the candidate for the
   harness's own mistake and reverted** — verified, pristine `34357e49…` back in
   service with the serving script restored. Correct behaviour, 40 minutes, and
   the run had been measuring the CPU recurrence rather than the regime every
   recorded number was taken in. CLAUDE.md names both the 1695 caps and the
   8-thread pinning; the block now copies `p9_gate.sh` verbatim instead of
   paraphrasing it.
2. `local label=$1 n=$2 dir="…$label"` — bash creates every name in one `local`
   before assigning, so `$label` is unbound under `set -u`.
3. The seed identified the tool block from the log's `CKPT hit` lines, and every
   restart truncates that log (`> $LOG`). It now *makes* the evidence: one UI
   turn against the gateway already up.
4. **P10's own persistence defeated its control arm.** Skipping the warm-ups
   does not make an arm cold when the counter is on disk — both arms loaded
   `hits = 4` and both kept the tool block, discriminating nothing. The cold arm
   now truncates the trailing counter off, which is byte-for-byte a file written
   by the pre-P10 binary. **A control that cannot fail is not a control**, and
   this one announced itself only because the gate checked that the control
   failed.

### What P10 does not fix

`hits` is a lifetime count with no decay: a prefix popular last month keeps its
score. With four slots and one dominant prefix that is the wanted behaviour; an
aging term (halve every N stores) is the follow-up and the field is already
there to age. Nothing here makes a *new* prefix cheaper — a 3 500-token paste is
still minutes — and four slots is still four.

## G5 (2026-09-11): the pooled DSA block keys are cached — the indexer's constant drops ~2.5x, not to zero

Ported the *idea* from Qwen's `2d3cf7e`, not the code, per CLAUDE.md and this
item's own instructions — the two indexers are different code (Qwen pools by a
fixed block ratio and RMSNorm+RoPEs the average; GLM pools by `index_kpool` and
a per-channel softmax mixture over the pool's gate logits, §"Long context"
above and `sparse_index.h`'s header comment) but the same shape of waste:
`coli_sparse_index_select_range` (`sparse_index.h:90`ff) recomputes every
pool's mixed key from its raw member keys/gates on *every* call, even though
the mix depends only on the pool's own members and its fixed intra-pool
positions (`ape`) — never on the querying token. Once a pool is COMPLETE (all
`index_kpool` members present, causally behind the current sequence length)
its mix never changes again, and GLM's KV is append-only, so a pool that
closes stays closed. Exactly Qwen's insight, ported to GLM's pooling function
instead of its RoPE'd block average.

**What did NOT get cached, on purpose, matching Qwen's own admission:** the
per-query score dot-product (`sum_h head_w[h] * relu(query·pooled[p])`) is
genuinely query-dependent and stays O(pools) per token — "not something
caching can remove" in Qwen's own words. So this is a constant-factor cut to
the indexer's O(context) slope, not a change of complexity class: **G5's own
roadmap row was right that this is the only O(context²)-total component, and
still is** after this change — the total work across a full generation is
still quadratic, just with a smaller leading constant now that the mixing
half of it is amortized to O(1) per token instead of paid again on every call.

**The guard C1 required.** Sizing the cache buffer at session-open time
divides by `c->index_kpool`; a real checkpoint always validates
`index_kpool >= 1` at load (`glm53.c` ~line 323, the same shape of guarantee
Qwen's `Q38_NEED` gives `idx_ratio`), but a Model built directly rather than
parsed — a fabricated test struct, the exact class of input that SIGFPE'd
`ensure_kv` in `2d3cf7e` — could leave it 0. Guarded the same way `df8ddc5`
guarded Qwen's `ensure_kv`: `index_kpool > 0 ? (cap+index_kpool-1)/index_kpool
: cap`, a generous fallback that is never actually read (the indexer itself
already refuses `pool<1` before touching the cache).

**Where the state lives, and how it resets.** Unlike Qwen (one persistent
`Model` with four explicit `q38_ik_pool_reset()` call sites), GLM already
allocates a fresh `GLayerState` array per `GSession`, `calloc`'d — so the new
`pool_cache_count` field starts at 0 for free on every ordinary "new session"
path (`slot_reset`+`session_open` on a prompt that does not extend the
slot's own prefix, the CLI's one session per process, the segment adapter's
one session per `glm53_segment_session_create`). The two places a session's
position content gets overwritten from OUTSIDE `mla_layer`'s own forward
pass — `ckpt_restore` (P7's checkpoint restore) and
`glm53_segment_session_restore` (cross-host snapshot restore) — do not carry
`pool_cache` in their span lists, so they explicitly zero `pool_cache_count`
for the DSA layers they touch after copying the checkpoint bytes in, the
same belt-and-suspenders reset Qwen's four call sites are. In both cases the
session was freshly opened immediately before the restore call anyway (so
the count was already 0), but the explicit reset documents the invariant
and survives a future refactor that stops guaranteeing it.

**Knob:** `GLM53_NO_INDEX_CACHE=1` reproduces the old, always-recompute path
(`coli_sparse_index_select_range`, byte-for-byte unchanged) — named after the
existing `GLM53_NO_MMAP` / `GLM53_NO_CANCEL_POLL` disable knobs. Default is
the new cached path. Not added to `docs/ENVIRONMENT.md`: that file documents
only `upstream/dev`'s knobs, and this fork's other `GLM53_*` additions
(`GLM53_I4_FAST` etc.) are not there either, per existing convention.

### Oracle: bit-identical at both sizes, cache ON and OFF

Pristine binary built aside first (`~/bench/glm53.g5base`, sha
`fe5dd4b…b60338`, the exact binary in service before this item) from the
unmodified tree at `c4a3e68`, so the A/B is against the same tree minus this
change. Candidate built in a separate worktree (`~/src/colibri-g5`), same
commit, this item's patch applied. `GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT` unset (CLI path never
touches it), 3 GPUs, `COLI_VK_EXPERTS2=1695 COLI_VK_EXPERTS3=1695`, 8 threads
pinned, page cache 100% resident (`fincore` across all 62 shards, per
RP1-CORRECTION's corrected procedure), `--greedy 8 --logits` so the same run
gives `teacher_forcing` over the whole prefill AND a decode-phase `[OPTIME]`
reading:

| prompt | tokens | `teacher_forcing` pristine vs candidate | `last_logits` | candidate vs `GLM53_NO_INDEX_CACHE=1` |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| `prefill_prompt_600.txt` | 781 | **IDENTICAL** | **IDENTICAL** (diff empty) | `teacher_forcing`/`last_logits` **IDENTICAL** to pristine too |
| `g5_prompt_1642.txt` (809-word prefix of `prefill_prompt_3000.txt`, built this item — RP1's own 690/1642 sizes are Qwen's, not GLM's; these are the closest this item measured, "roughly" per its own gate text) | 1509 | **IDENTICAL** | **IDENTICAL** (diff empty) | not re-run at this size; the mechanism is the same function at both, and the 781-token leg already isolates the knob |

Zero-tolerance diff (`diff`, no epsilon) on both the argmax stream and the
full last-token logit vector — this is a caching change, not a numerics
change, and there is no excuse for drift. `GLM53_NO_INDEX_CACHE=1`'s own
`[OPTIME]` reading at 781 tokens (`index/ctx=1.9984 us`, `index=1.570 ms/call`)
reproduces pristine's (`1.9721 us`, `1.549 ms/call`) within run-to-run noise,
confirming the knob is really routing to the old, unchanged function and not
just coincidentally producing the same output by a different path.

### `[OPTIME] mla split`, decode phase only (`optime_reset()` right before
### `--greedy`), 8 threads, 3 GPUs, mean ctx as printed

| prompt (tokens) | binary | proj ms/call | **index ms/call** | attn ms/call | **index/ctx µs** |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 781 (mean ctx 786) | pristine | 1.112 | **1.549** | 2.255 | **1.9721** |
| 781 (mean ctx 786) | candidate | 1.123 | **0.615** | 2.263 | **0.7829** |
| 1509 (mean ctx 1514) | pristine | 1.125 | **3.137** | 2.882 | **2.0724** |
| 1509 (mean ctx 1514) | candidate | 1.102 | **1.312** | 2.905 | **0.8669** |

`index/ctx` — the per-context-token, per-call constant RP1 fit — drops
**1.97→0.78 µs (2.52×)** at ctx≈786 and **2.07→0.87 µs (2.39×)** at ctx≈1514:
consistent within noise across a ~2× range of context depth, which is what a
genuine constant-factor cut (not a fit artifact) should look like. `proj` and
`attn` are unchanged (neither touches the indexer), which is the expected
negative control.

Per-token saving at the two measured depths (×11 DSA layers/token):
ctx≈786: (1.549−0.615)×11 = **10.3 ms/token**; ctx≈1514:
(3.137−1.312)×11 = **20.1 ms/token** — both from a single per-op timer, not
extrapolated. Prefill wall time is within noise of pristine at both sizes
(298.8s→298.4s at 781 tokens, 584.3s→583.1s at 1509) because at these depths
the indexer is still a small share of a CPU-expert-dominated prefill —
exactly the "nothing at short/medium context" the roadmap row already said,
now measured rather than modelled.

**What this does and does not do to the crossover table.** §"Long context"
above and its RP1 correction disagree with each other on the CPU-expert
bucket's size (74.8 vs RP1-CORRECTION's 40.3 ms/token) and therefore on where
the indexer was said to overtake it (≈3.5k vs a smaller number neither
section recomputed) — not a disagreement this item's numbers can settle, and
picking one silently here would just add a third guess. What this item's own
measurement supports directly: the indexer's slope is now **~2.4–2.5× smaller
than whatever crossover ctx the older sections used**, so every crossover
figure in this file moves out by roughly that factor, without changing which
side of any of them decode traffic on this box actually reaches. G5 remains
what §G8 and RP1 already called it — a product question about deployed
context length — and this change makes the answer to that question matter
even less at any context this rig has been asked to run.

### Serving-regime sanity (`rome_bench.sh`, short-context rotating median,
### mean ctx ≈106 where RP1 already measured G5 at 1.1% of the token)

| run | pristine | candidate |
|---|---:|---:|
| 1 | 3.08 tok/s | 3.10 tok/s |
| 2 | 3.02 tok/s | 3.08 tok/s |

Within run-to-run noise (~2%), as expected — this item is not worth anything
at the context this regime measures, and the sanity check's job is only to
confirm it costs nothing there either. It does not.

### Verdict: ships, on by default

Bit-identical oracle at both sizes and both knob positions, a real and
reproducible cut to the one genuinely quadratic-total component's constant,
zero measured cost at short context, and the change is entirely local to
`sparse_index.h`'s new function plus per-session bookkeeping in `glm53.c` —
no shared header, so `qwen38`/`qwen38-vk` were rebuilt (`make -C c qwen38
qwen38-vk glm53 VK=1`, all clean) but not re-measured; nothing in
`sparse_index.h`'s existing, unchanged functions or in `qwen38_core.h`
changed. All four `qwen38` C tests still pass; `test_glm53_cancel_frames`
and `test_glm53_ckpt_touch` still pass. `tests/test_sparse_index` could not
be run in this checkout — its fixture (`glm53_indexer_case.h`) is generated
by `c/tools/make_glm53_tiny.py` from a `torch`/`transformers` oracle and is
not checked in or present on this rig; pre-existing gap, not introduced or
worsened by this item, and the engine-level `teacher_forcing`/`last_logits`
oracle above already exercises the changed code path directly against a
real checkpoint rather than a synthetic case. Recommend **on by default**:
there is no downside at any measured context and a genuine, growing-with-context
win above the roadmap's own crossover analysis — the only reason this was
ever "last" was priority, not risk, and the owner's request settles the
priority question. Not run through `accept_live.sh`/`accept_ui.sh` — the CLI
oracle path never touches the gateway, `GLM53_PREFIX_CKPT`, or Open WebUI's
serving path, and this item's own gate does not call for it; whoever next
promotes a binary into service should still run the full serving chain as
usual.
