## Q-REBASE (2026-09-11): the Track Q baseline, re-taken — and Track G's two weeks of shared-file work are neutral on Qwen

Every Q row in this file predates the kernel work of the prefill and G tracks.
`qwen38-vk` links `c/backend_vulkan.c`, `c/quant.h` and `c/shaders/*`, and P3,
P4/P4b/P4c, P5.2, RP4, G11, G12, G13 and G14 all changed one of those. Nothing
re-measured Qwen afterwards, so the 09-06 and 09-09 rows were historical, not a
comparison point. `tools/rome_bench.sh qwen38-vk`, cap 512, max-new 80, 8
threads, GPU tier asserted by the harness, binary sha256 `24a075ee4116326e`.

| run | rotating median | warm-identical | cold |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| q-rebase-0911-evict-run1 | **4.04** | 5.23 | 3.45 |
| q-rebase-0911-evict-run2 | **4.09** | 5.25 | 3.37 |
| q-rebase-0911-noevict-run1 | 4.46 | 5.30 | 4.16 |
| q-rebase-0911-noevict-run2 | 4.88 | 5.30 | 4.80 |
| *2026-09-04 gpu-real1 / 2* | *4.12 / 4.13* | *5.43 / 5.44* | *3.47 / 3.47* |
| *2026-09-06 q-baseline-run1 / 2* | *4.06 / 4.10* | *5.45 / 5.46* | *2.32 / 2.40* |
| *2026-09-09 q-remap-run1 / 2* | *4.17 / 4.31* | *5.25 / 5.25* | *3.38 / 3.44* |

**The two evicting rows are the baseline** — same eviction as every historical
row here. They land on top of 09-06 (−0.5% and −0.2%) and 2–5% under 09-09,
whose own two runs differ by 3.4% from each other; warm-identical reproduces
09-09's 5.25 exactly and cold reproduces 09-09's and 09-04's. **Two weeks of
shared-file kernel work moved nothing on this engine, on any of the three
columns, beyond the harness's own noise.**

That is not luck, and it was checked in the code rather than inferred:

- `vk_tile_ok4` (`backend_vulkan.c`) requires `S > 1 && (fmt == 1 || fmt == 4)`.
  Qwen's experts are `fmt=8` and decode is `S=1`, so **P4/P4b/P4c's tiled
  pipelines are never selected on this engine in either phase.**
- `c/quant.h` since 2026-09-04 is **purely additive** — 146 lines added, 0
  removed, all of them G11/G14 int4 helpers. `matmul_fp8`, the kernel qwen38
  actually runs, is untouched.
- `coli_vk_kda_pool_init` (P6b's per-slot VRAM pool) is called from `glm53.c`
  only. qwen38 never calls it, so it takes no VRAM from Qwen's tier — and
  indeed the tier came up at 14 673 with dev0 at 24.4/25.7 GB, the same figures
  as 09-04, 09-06 and 09-09.
- `COLI_VK_TIMESTAMPS` (RP4) is off by default.

**The no-evict rows are NOT a baseline and are recorded only so nobody re-runs
them expecting one:** 4.46 vs 4.88 is a 9.4% spread, and the per-request tables
put it in expert disk service (3.256 s vs 0.624 s per request). Use the
evicting rows.

### Two corrections this campaign forced

**1. `rome_bench.sh`'s `sudo -n drop_caches` fails on this box and that does not
matter.** All four runs printed "Warning: Cache eviction requires sudo
password". The eviction the cold column actually measures is
`datapoint.py`'s own `evict_cache` — `posix_fadvise(DONTNEED)` over all 131
shards, no sudo needed — which runs before every run unless `--no-evict`. The
sudo step is a pre-warm nicety. A reader of the run log would otherwise
conclude these rows were taken warm; they were not.

**2. The Qwen tier is VRAM-limited, not history-limited.** Seven separate
processes on 2026-09-11 all printed `Vulkan tier preloaded 14673 of 21858 hot
experts ... (dev0 24.4/25.7 GB)` — identical to the digit. The histogram
*offers* 21 858 candidates and the preload stops at 14 673 because the cards are
full (14 673 × 4.92 MB ≈ 72 GB, which is all three of them). The roadmap's Q0
said "capture a longer routing history so the preload fills VRAM (14 673 of
~14 700 slots)"; **there are no empty slots and more history cannot add an
expert.** This kills half of Q0 outright and is why Q8 (a smaller expert
format; Q6 in the pre-rewrite numbering) inherits its purpose.

## Q-PROFILE (2026-09-11): where a Qwen3.8 decode token goes — the G3-equivalent Track Q never had

Track Q had no per-op profile, so its six items were ordered by guesswork and
the roadmap said so. This is the profile. It is the Qwen twin of §G3: real
`[OPTIME]` timers that **partition** the token, plus a `perf` flat profile as
the independent cross-check, plus an accounting residual printed by the engine
itself so a missing timer cannot hide.

**What already existed, and why it could not produce this table.** `COLI_TIMERS`
has been read in `qwen38_core.h` since well before this branch, and ten counters
(`expert-read`, `fp8-expand`, `routed-expert`, `shared-expert`, `resident-mm`,
`deltanet`, `qsa-index`, `qsa-attn`, `ple`, `lm-head`) were already wired, with
a per-request delta (`q38_tm_delta`) that serve mode prints. They are not a
profile and the report said so in its own footer ("architecture phases overlap
resident-mm"): the gated residual, the router, the top-k and the embedding
gather were **untimed**, `resident-mm` **cross-cuts** DeltaNet and QSA, and the
GPU wait was folded into `routed-expert` together with the CPU share that runs
beside it. Ten new counters were added rather than built from scratch — `step`
as the denominator, `embed`/`gr-read`/`gr-apply`/`attention`/`moe` to complete
the partition, and `router`/`cpu-experts`/`vk-issue`/`vk-take` to decompose the
MoE — plus a prefill snapshot so the table is decode-only. They cost nothing
when `COLI_TIMERS` is unset (`q38_tm_live`), unlike the ten older ones, which
pay a `clock_gettime` unconditionally.

**Regime.** Fresh process, 3 GPUs, tier 14 673 of 21 858 (dev0 24.4/25.7 GB),
cap 512, 8 threads pinned, routing history frozen to a copy for the whole
campaign, page cache asserted 100% resident on all 131 shards before every run,
`datapoint.py`'s rotating prompt 1 (30 tokens), 80 greedy tokens, mean context
≈70. Three repeats.

### The table (decode only, 191.2 ms/token; three runs agree to 0.5% on every row)

| op | ms/token | % | calls/token | runs on | notes |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| **DeltaNet (36 layers)** | **70.5** | **36.9** | 36 | mixed | 1.96 ms/call; ~55 of it is its own projections (below) |
| **MoE (48 layers)** | **56.4** | **29.5** | 48 | mixed | fully decomposed below (remainder ~1.0 ms) |
| — vk-issue (placement + staging + 3 submits) | 17.1 | 8.9 | 48 | 1 thread | ~7.6 ms of it is the `q38vk_expert_ensure` + `memcpy` staging loop |
| — CPU experts | 15.4 | 8.1 | 92 experts | 8 threads | runs **inside** the issue→take gap, so it is hidden |
| — vk-take (the **residual** GPU wait) | 13.0 | 6.8 | 48 | 1 thread | pure idle after the CPU share is exhausted |
| — shared expert | 7.2 | 3.8 | 48 | 8 threads | CPU, and it runs **before** the issue — see Q3 |
| — router | 2.7 | 1.4 | 48 | 8 threads | 512×2560 matmul + a scalar top-10-of-512 with an O(K²) dedup scan |
| **gated residual read (97 sites)** | **26.7** | **14.0** | 97 | mixed | 0.276 ms/site; ~10 of it single-threaded scalar |
| **QSA attention (12 layers)** | **20.5** | **10.7** | 12 | mixed | of which qsa-attn 4.4 and qsa-index 0.15 at ctx≈70 |
| **lm-head** | **15.8** | **8.2** | 1 | 8 threads | 1.27 GB at **80.4 GB/s** |
| PLE | 1.12 | 0.6 | 1 | | |
| gated residual apply (96 sites) | 0.18 | 0.1 | 96 | 1 thread | |
| embed | 0.04 | 0.0 | 1 | | |
| **unaccounted** | **0.04** | **0.02** | | | sum 191.78 vs step 191.83 |

Cross-cutting counters (**nested**, do not add them to the total):
`dense-matmul` **90.0 ms/token (47.0%)**, `expert-read` 6.0 (3.2%),
`fp8-expand` 0.000. Routed-expert placement: **30 641 GPU / 7 279 CPU =
80.80% of routed activations served by the tier** (480 per token = 48 layers ×
top-10; 388 GPU, 92 CPU).

### perf flat, `cycles:u` — 238k samples over 30 s of decode

`qwen38-vk` **67.32%**, `libgomp` **31.75%**, `libm` 0.33%, `libc` 0.21%.
By symbol: `q38_matmul_bf16._omp_fn.0` **30.49%**, libgomp's unresolved spin
addresses **31.2%** combined, `q38_dense_matmul_multi._omp_fn.0` **24.91%**,
`matmul_fp8._omp_fn.0` 5.54%, `q38_deltanet._omp_fn.0` 4.62%,
`step.constprop.0` 1.16% (it inlines the gated residual's scalar loops),
`q38_gr_read.constprop.0` 0.39%, `__expf_fma` 0.30%.

**Cross-validation.** The two BF16 kernels are **55.40%** of user cycles. The
timers put `dense-matmul` at 46.5% of wall **plus** `lm-head` at 8.2% — the
`dense-matmul` counter excludes the LM head, which goes through
`q38_weight_matmul` rather than `q38_dense_matmul` — for **54.7%**. The two
instruments are **0.7 percentage points apart**, the same class of agreement
§G3 obtained for GLM.

**The single largest structural fact, and it is the opposite of GLM's.**
31.75% of cycles are OpenMP barrier spin, so ≈31.75 × 8/7 = **36.3% of the
decode token runs on one core** (~70 ms). §G3 measured **69%** for GLM-5.3.
Qwen is already half as serial, because the thing that dominates its token is an
8-thread BF16 GEMV rather than a scalar router and a single-threaded recurrence.
The corollary matters more than the number: **the cheap parallelism wins that
carried track G from 1.65 to 2.75 tok/s are mostly already taken here.**

### The bandwidth arithmetic, which decides the whole plan

Bytes streamed per decode token, from `config.json`'s shapes (hidden 2560,
hc_count 4 → widened 10 240, hc_lowrank 320, 36 DeltaNet + 12 QSA layers,
512 experts × top-10, moe_intermediate 640, vocab 248 320), all BF16:

| through | per layer | per token |
|---|---:|---:|
| DeltaNet projections (qkv 2560×10240, z 2560×6144, out 6144×2560) ×36 | 115.4 MB | **4.16 GB** |
| QSA projections (q 2560×12288, k/v, idx_qk, o 6144×2560) ×12 | 103 MB | **1.24 GB** |
| gated residual (down 10240×320, up 320×10240, inject) ×97 sites | 13.2 MB | **1.28 GB** |
| router (512×2560) ×48 | 2.6 MB | 0.13 GB |
| **total through the `dense-matmul` counter** | | **6.81 GB** |
| lm-head (2560×248320), counted separately | | **1.27 GB** |

6.81 GB in 90.0 ms = **75.7 GB/s**. 1.27 GB in 15.8 ms = **80.4 GB/s**. This
box's measured DRAM read bandwidth is **70.9 GB/s at 8 threads** and 91.6 at 16
(§The machine). **So 105.8 ms/token — 55% of the decode token — is already
running at or above the machine's 8-thread memory ceiling, and no CPU-side
change can move it.** That is the measured version of what the 09-04 backlog
called "dense set off DRAM", and it is now the only bucket on this engine where
a different device is the answer rather than an optimisation.

### What is left, and it is ~85 ms

Subtracting the bandwidth-bound share of each bucket at 75.7 GB/s — **this split
is arithmetic from the shapes above, not a timer, and every item below is
therefore required to put a sub-timer in first** (§G4 is the precedent: §G3's
KDA decomposition missed 1.05 ms/call of GPU submits and only a sub-timer
found it):

| what | ms/token | why it is there |
|---|---:|---|
| DeltaNet serial scalar (est. 70.5 − 55.0) | **~15.9** | the `CD=10 240`-channel causal conv — a silu and a 3-tap shift per channel, **serial**, 36 layers = 368 640 iterations/token — plus the per-head q/k normalisation with `double` accumulators and the per-head output RMSNorm. Only the 48-head recurrence has a `parallel for`. |
| CPU experts | 15.4 | 92 experts × 4.92 MB = 453 MB at **29.4 GB/s = 41% of DRAM** — the same ALU/decode-bound shape §G3 found in GLM's int4 kernel at 27% |
| vk-take, pure GPU wait | 13.0 | the shared expert (7.2) sits on the wrong side of the issue |
| gated residual serial scalar (est. 26.7 − 16.9) | **~9.9** | 4 × `q38_rms0` with a `double` accumulator over H=2560, plus a C=4 × H=2560 sigmoid mix — **≈1M `expf` per token, single-threaded**, 97 times per token. This is §G10's mHC bucket in a different engine. |
| submit calls | ~9.5 | |
| staging memcpy + `q38vk_expert_ensure` | ~7.6 | |
| QSA non-projection (est. 20.5 − 16.4) | ~4.1 | qsa-attn alone is 4.4 at ctx≈70 and **11.1 at ctx≈180** — the one O(context) term here |
| router top-k, accumulate, PLE, rest | ~9 | |

### Context scaling, measured on the perf run (300 tokens, mean ctx ≈180)

`qsa-attn` 4.4 → **11.1 ms/token**, `qsa-index` 0.148 → 0.276, `attention`
20.5 → 27.4; every other bucket is flat within 1%. The indexer — the component
§G5 spent a day on for GLM — is **0.15 ms/token at ctx≈70 and 0.28 at ctx≈180**
here, and Qwen already has the pooled-key cache (`IK_pooled`, commit `2d3cf7e`,
the very commit G5 ported *from*). It is not a Track Q item at any context this
box will run.

### Method notes, and one constant worth having

- **Every `qwen38-vk` process drops the model's page-cache residency from
  100.0000% to 94.3699%**, reproducibly to four decimals across seven runs, and
  it does not come back. GLM's constant is 91.6347% (§RP2). Re-warm and assert
  before every run; `tools/hot-expert/q_profile_run.sh` does.
- **qwen38 reads `COLI_USAGE`, not `COLI_USAGE_PATH`**, defaults it to
  `<snap>/.coli_usage`, and rewrites it at exit. `rome_bench.sh` freezes the
  histogram for glm53 and **not** for qwen38, so every Q row ever recorded
  mutated the history the next row preloaded from. It did not bite — the tier is
  VRAM-limited and landed on 14 673 in every run — but that is luck. The
  profiling chain freezes it.
- **The instrumentation is bit-identical.** Against the pristine
  `24a075ee4116326e` (the binary the Q-REBASE rows above were taken on), same
  frozen history, same tier, same shaders: last-token logits **byte-identical**
  (993 280 bytes, i.e. all 248 320 floats) and greedy text identical, both with
  `COLI_TIMERS` unset and with it set to 1. The expert cache hit rate matched to
  the digit in all three runs (30.8%, hit=6940 miss=15627).

### The isolated microbenchmark, and the three places it changed the plan

`tools/hot-expert/rome_cpubench.c` unchanged (the harness that produced the
09-04 placement matrix: the engine's own kernels, working sets rotated through
pools larger than the 128 MB L3, so every figure is DRAM-streaming), rebuilt
against today's `quant.h`, **8 threads pinned**, rig lock held, no engine
running:

```
BF16 engine (1 acc)  S= 1  2560x10240    0.658 ms  79.6 GB/s
BF16 acc4            S= 1  2560x10240    0.577 ms  90.8 GB/s
BF16 engine (1 acc)  S= 1  2560x6144     0.385 ms  81.8 GB/s
BF16 acc4            S= 1  2560x6144     0.349 ms  90.2 GB/s
BF16 engine (1 acc)  S= 1  6144x2560     0.390 ms  80.7 GB/s
BF16 acc4            S= 1  6144x2560     0.358 ms  87.9 GB/s
BF16 engine (1 acc)  S= 1  2560x248320  15.011 ms  84.7 GB/s   <- LM head
BF16 acc4            S= 1  2560x248320  13.710 ms  92.7 GB/s
INT8 maddubs         S= 1  2560x10240    0.280 ms  93.6 GB/s
FP8 routed experts K=10 engine-loop   1.602 ms/token-layer (0.16 ms/expert, 30.7 GB/s)
FP8 routed experts K=10 fused-region  1.387 ms/token-layer (0.14 ms/expert, 35.4 GB/s)
```

**1. In-engine attenuation on this engine's dense path is ~0.95×, not §G14's
0.55–0.65×.** The LM head is one tensor of one shape and goes through one
kernel, so it is the clean comparison: **isolated 15.011 ms, in-engine
15.8 ms/token.** The `dense-matmul` counter's 75.7 GB/s against the isolated
79.6–84.7 GB/s says the same thing for the rest. §G14's attenuation figure was
for a path streaming **cold mmap'd expert weights**; these are resident BF16
dense tensors read every token, and they carry no such penalty. **Do not port
§G14's 0.55–0.65× to this engine's dense items.**

**2. The dense stream is NOT purely bandwidth-bound, and the old Q5 was
mis-scoped.** The 4-accumulator variant is **1.10–1.14× at S=1** on every dense
shape this engine uses (79.6→90.8, 81.8→90.2, 80.7→87.9, 84.7→92.7 GB/s). The
roadmap's old Q5 row read "4-accumulator BF16 matmul for prefill rows (S>1
only) … TTFT, not decode", sized only from the S=32 figure. **At S=1 it is
worth 10–14% of 105.8 ms/token**, i.e. it is a decode item and always was. It
changes the summation order, so it is not bit-identical and ships behind a knob
— but 09-04's "two FMA chains, no change, logits differed for nothing" was
measured on `matmul_fp8`, the expert kernel, and does **not** cover this one.

**3. The CPU expert path has an un-ported §G11 part 1.** Isolated, the same ten
experts cost **1.602 ms/token-layer through the engine's loop and 1.387 fused
into one OpenMP region (1.16×)**. `q38_moe_decode`'s CPU share runs three
separate `q38_weight_matmul` calls per expert — **3 OMP regions × 92 CPU
experts = 276 fork/joins per token**. §G11 part 1 did exactly this fusion for
GLM, bit-identically, for −2.25 ms/token.

Two cross-validations worth keeping, both of which say the measurement is sound:

- **CPU experts, isolated vs in-engine: 0.16 vs 0.167 ms/expert, 30.7 vs
  29.4 GB/s — 4% apart.** The mmap/prefault work on the original branch really
  did remove the 4–6× page-fault penalty the 09-04 §"Where the Qwen CPU token
  went" section found; nothing is left of it.
- **INT8 dense is 0.280 ms against BF16's 0.658 at 2560×10240** — 2.35× per
  call, half the bytes at a higher rate. That is the CPU-side half of the Q7
  (dense off DRAM) decision and it costs numerics, exactly as the 09-04 matrix
  said.

## Q-ARB (2026-09-11): the VRAM split between the dense stream and the expert tier — decided by a rule and a probe, not negotiated

§Q-PROFILE left one design question open and named it for Fable: Q7 (the
dense BF16 stream off the CPU's DRAM floor) and Q8 (FP8 → int4 experts for
tier density) both want the same ~72 GB of VRAM, from opposite directions.
This section is the arbitration. It rests on nothing that was not already in
§Q-PROFILE, §Q-REBASE, the 09-04 placement matrix and the engine source; it
adds arithmetic and a decision.

### Three facts from the source that the decision needs

- **Budget.** `q38vk_expert_ensure` places an expert on dev0, else dev2, else
  dev3, and each device stops when `budget − used < 1.5 GB`. 3 × 25.7 − 3 × 1.5
  ≈ 72.6 GB less scratch; the tier measured 14 673 × 4.92 MB = 72.2 GB.
- **Fill order.** `q38vk_preload` sorts every candidate by routing count and
  places hottest-first across the three devices in that order. So the coldest
  resident experts are on dev3, and **any VRAM allocated before the preload
  evicts exactly the coldest experts with no placement code** — which is what
  makes the eviction probe (QP(d)) a zero-code measurement.
- **The int4 expert is not 2.46 MB.** 4 915 200 weights → 2.46 MB of nibbles
  plus 76 800 g64 scales at 2 or 4 bytes = **2.61–2.76 MB**. All 24 576 are
  64–68 GB; the 21 858 with history are 57–60 GB.

### The per-GB rule

A GB of dense BF16 costs **13.2 ms/token** on the CPU (6.81 GB in 90.0 ms at
75.7 GB/s) and ~2–3 ms on the GPU — about **−10 ms/token per GB moved**.

A GB at the cold end of the tier is 203 FP8 experts. The coldest resident
expert serves at most the resident mean (80.8% / 14 673 = 0.0055% of
activations) and at least the unplaced mean (19.2% / 7 185 = 0.0027%), so a
GB of them is ≤ 1.1% of activations, ≤ 5.4 more CPU experts per token, ≤ **0.9
ms/token** of CPU-expert time — and that time runs inside the issue→take gap,
which after Q3 still has ~5.8 ms/token of idle, while every expert moved off
the GPU shortens the GPU side by 0.073 ms (28.4 ms of gap for 388 experts).

**Dense weight is worth ≥ 10× more per GB than the marginal expert. The dense
set is reserved on dev0 first; the tier fills what remains.** This holds in
both cases below, so it is a rule and not a trade.

### The ledger

Dense set: DeltaNet 4.16 + QSA 1.24 + gated residual 1.28 = 6.68 GB BF16
(3.34 int8); LM head 1.27 BF16 / 0.64 int8; router (0.13 GB, 1.7 ms/token)
stays on the CPU because 48 submits cost more than it does.

| case | dense on dev0 | left for experts | placed | evicted vs today |
|---|---:|---:|---|---|
| A. Q8 alive, int4 g64 | 6.68 + 0.64 = 7.3 GB | 64.9 GB | 24 850 at 2.61 MB → **all 24 576**; 23 500 at 2.76 MB → **all 21 858 with history** | tier grows ~8 800 |
| B. Q8 dead, DeltaNet + QSA BF16 | 5.4 GB | 66.8 GB | 13 570 | ~1 100 coldest (7.5%) |
| B. Q8 dead, whole dense set BF16 | 6.7 GB | 65.5 GB | 13 300 | ~1 380 coldest (9.4%) |
| B. Q8 dead, int8 `fmt` if the probe allows | 3.3 GB | 68.9 GB | 14 000 | ~670 (4.6%) |

Case A closes with the dense set first in either scale width. Case B's
eviction, by the bounds above, costs 2.9–7.6% of activations → 14–36 more CPU
experts/token → 2.3–6.1 ms of CPU-expert time, of which ~0–2 ms reaches the
fresh-process token, and at most +8 to +22 ms/token on the rotating median if
the 56 ms miss surplus (247 ms rotating vs 191 warm-identical) scales linearly
with the miss share — against −35 to −50 ms/token from the move. Net positive
in every row. The bound is replaced by a measurement before the Q7 spec is
written: `Q38_VK_BALLAST_GB` at 3.4, 5.4 and 6.8 through `rome_bench.sh`.

### The decision

1. **Not one co-designed document.** With the ledger in hand the two items
   share one unknown — does Qwen's expert survive int4 g64 — and QP(a)
   answers it in a day, the way §G15 answered int3 for GLM. The Q7 spec is
   then written against one ledger row. The old roadmap's mistake here was
   not "designing alone": it paired dense-off-DRAM with MTP, which needs
   verify bandwidth rather than VRAM, and left the real VRAM competitor
   "(later)".
2. **Q7 has two arms and the probe picks.** Q7-cpu: int8 dense weights
   quantised at load through the `maddubs` kernel `rome_cpubench.c` already
   has — 0.280 vs 0.658 ms at 2560×10240, ~2.2× in-engine, **−50 to −60
   ms/token including the LM head, no VRAM, 1–2 days**. Q7-gpu: the dense
   projections on dev0 as a BF16 `fmt` (order-only numerics) or int8, dense
   allocated before the preload — **−35 to −50 against today's CPU figure,
   but only −14 to −20 after Q7-cpu** (2.7 GB of int8 at ~425 GB/s batched is
   7–10 ms on dev0 against ~29 ms on the CPU at 93.6 GB/s).
3. **Order.** Q3 → Q1 → Q2 (bit-identical) → **QP** → Q5 → Fable session →
   then by the probe: int8 dense survives → Q7-cpu → Q8 (if alive) → Q9, with
   Q7-gpu only if a re-profile after Q7-cpu still shows ≥ 15 ms/token in the
   dense stream; int8 dense dies → Q4 → Q8 (if alive) → Q7-gpu at BF16 with
   the ballast rows as its measured VRAM cost → Q9. Q8 precedes Q7-gpu in
   every branch because it de-contends the VRAM and because it is the only
   item that acts on the 56 ms rotating surplus at all.
4. **Q8's size is the rotating/warm gap, not the CPU-expert bucket.** The
   15.4 ms of CPU experts is hidden inside the GPU wait (13.0 ms of idle
   remain after it); removing it moves a counter. Q8's fresh-process value
   is −10 to −15 (staging loop, shorter int4 gap); its rotating value is up
   to −56. The same fact re-scopes Q5 to ~0 on the fresh-process token.

The roadmap's Track Q section carries the item rows, the 2×2 decision table
and what each spec must contain.

## Q3 (2026-09-11): the shared expert into the GPU gap — bit-identical, −4.1 ms/token, the gate as written NARROWLY NOT MET; merged 2026-09-12 by arbitration (`### Arbitration` at the end of this section)

§G9's pattern ported to `qwen38-vk`. In `q38_moe_decode` the order was
router → shared expert (CPU, 8 threads) → stage/issue the routed groups →
CPU share of the routed experts in the gap → take. The shared expert is
7.2 ms/token of pure CPU work with no dependency on anything the GPUs read,
and it sat entirely **before** the issue, so none of it overlapped the
13.0 ms/token of pure idle §Q-PROFILE measured in `vk-take`. **The issue now
runs first and the shared expert runs inside the gap**, between the issue and
the CPU share.

`c/qwen38_core.h` only — the `Q38_VK_TIER` block is cut in two by the shared
expert and the `bufG/bufU/bufD/bufRows/bufZ/bufN/bufX/bufY` statics,
`placed_mask` and `issued` are hoisted one scope so both halves see them.
**Nothing shared with `glm53` is touched** (`quant.h`, `backend_vulkan.c`,
`c/shaders/*` unchanged; shader sha `813ffeaec6e25e5c` on both sides), so
GLM-5.3 was not rebuilt and does not need re-measuring.

**`ys` accumulation order is unchanged and that was the point:** the CPU share
still adds first, then device 0/1/2 in issue order, then the shared term last —
the shared expert is *computed* earlier, not *accumulated* earlier.

### Oracle — bit-identical, which is this item's gate

Pristine = `hot-expert-tier` @ `9a20b9c` built on the rig (sha256
`26474ee88e03b919`); candidate = `perf/q3-shared-expert-into-gap` @ `430ea84`
(sha256 `11299e43539af23d`). Same shaders, same frozen routing history copied
fresh per run, tier `14673 of 21858` in every run, 131 shards asserted 100%
resident before every run.

| pair | last-token logits (`DUMP`, 993 280 bytes = all 248 320 floats) | greedy text | expert cache |
|---|---|---|---|
| 32 tokens, timers off | **byte-identical** | identical | 30.8% hit=6940 miss=15627 both |
| 32 tokens, candidate with `COLI_TIMERS=1` | **byte-identical** | identical | identical |
| 128 tokens, timers off | **byte-identical** | identical | 60.0% hit=25654 miss=17097 both |

All four `qwen38` C tests exit 0 on the candidate. Both engines build clean.

### The isolated microbenchmark

`rome_cpubench.c`, 8 threads pinned, rig lock held, no engine running, pools
larger than the 128 MB L3. One new row: the shared expert is one
expert-shaped FP8 triple per layer (`shared_expert_intermediate_size ==
moe_intermediate_size == 640`), so `bench_fp8_experts(2560,640,K=1,...)` **is**
the op this item moves.

```
FP8 routed experts K=10 engine-loop   1.607 ms/token-layer (0.16 ms/expert, 30.6 GB/s)   [§Q-PROFILE: 1.602]
FP8 routed experts K=10 fused-region  1.394 ms/token-layer (0.14 ms/expert, 35.3 GB/s)   [§Q-PROFILE: 1.387]
FP8 routed experts K= 1 engine-loop   0.203 ms/token-layer (0.20 ms/expert, 24.2 GB/s)   x48 = 9.7 ms/token
```

The two K=10 rows reproduce §Q-PROFILE to 0.3% and 0.5%, so the harness and the
machine are in the same state. The new K=1 row predicts **9.7 ms/token**
against the engine's **7.2**: the isolated figure is the *slower* one, i.e. it
brackets the movable work from above. (The engine reads one contiguous 4.92 MB
triple per layer; the microbench rotates a 630 MB pool. The direction is the
safe one — this is not the "fast in L3, slow in the engine" failure this record
warns about, it is its opposite — and the gate is decided on the in-engine
counter, not on this row.)

### `[OPTIME]`, decode only, fresh process, three repeats each, medians

Same regime as §Q-PROFILE (`q3_chain.sh`, itself a copy of `q_profile_chain.sh`):
3 GPUs, cap 512, 80 greedy tokens, `datapoint.py`'s rotating prompt 1, 8 threads
pinned, history frozen to a fresh copy per run, residency asserted.

| op | pristine | candidate | delta |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| **step (the token)** | **190.47** | **186.62** | **−3.85** |
| **moe** | **55.90** | **51.75** | **−4.14** |
| — **vk-take** | **12.62** | **8.41** | **−4.21** |
| — shared | 7.20 | 7.34 | +0.15 |
| — cpu-experts | 15.33 | 15.56 | +0.23 |
| — vk-issue | 17.10 | 17.03 | −0.08 |
| — router | 2.70 | 2.75 | +0.04 |
| — routed-total | 37.18 | 32.92 | −4.27 |
| deltanet | 70.43 | 70.48 | +0.05 |
| gr-read | 26.84 | 26.74 | −0.09 |
| attention | 20.38 | 20.45 | +0.07 |
| lm-head | 15.73 | 15.73 | −0.01 |
| dense-matmul (nested) | 89.70 | 89.93 | +0.23 |
| expert-read (nested) | 6.03 | 6.03 | −0.00 |

Per-run spread: `moe` 55.82/55.92/55.90 → 51.75/51.70/51.97; `vk-take`
12.62/12.69/12.51 → **7.92/8.61/8.41**. Fresh-process `Speed:`
4.14/4.13/4.14 → **4.20/4.20/4.19 tok/s** (+1.5%); the timers-off oracle runs
agree (3.15→3.22 at 32 tokens, 4.48→4.56 at 128).

`expert-read` is flat to 0.00 ms, which answers the one thing that could have
gone wrong for free: the `posix_fadvise(WILLNEED)` prefetch used to get the
shared expert's 7.2 ms as a head start before the staging loop read the same
experts, and it no longer does. It cost nothing — the model is 100% resident,
which is the regime every recorded row is taken in.

### The gate: **NOT MET**, both numeric legs, narrowly

| gate leg | required | measured | verdict |
|---|---|---:|---|
| bit-identical, `ys` order unchanged | yes | logits byte-identical at 32 and 128 tokens | **met** |
| `[OPTIME] vk-take` | ≤ 8.0 ms/token | **8.41** (median; 7.92 / 8.61 / 8.41) | **not met** |
| `[OPTIME] moe` down | ≥ 5.0 ms/token | **4.14** | **not met** |

Expected was −5 to −7 ms/token; measured −3.85 on the token and −4.14 on `moe`.
**Reported, not tuned.** Nothing was adjusted after the number came in.

### Why it undershoots, in the measured numbers only

The post-issue window is `cpu-experts + vk-take` before and
`shared + cpu-experts + vk-take` after:

```
pristine   15.33 + 12.62                 = 27.95 ms/token
candidate   7.34 + 15.56 + 8.41          = 31.31 ms/token
```

7.34 ms of work moved into the window and the window grew by 3.36, so
**3.98 ms was absorbed — 54% of the shared expert**, which is the −4.14 on
`moe` to within the repeat spread. Per layer: the shared expert is
0.150 ms/layer, the pristine residual wait averages 0.263 ms/layer, and
0.088 ms/layer was recovered. The average is not the constraint; **the
distribution is.** The saving is `sum over layers of min(shared_l, wait_l)`,
and a layer whose CPU share is large (routing sends 92 of 480 activations to
the CPU per token, ~1.9 per layer but very unevenly) has already exhausted its
GPU wait before the shared expert can be spent in it. The unabsorbed
~3.4 ms/token is shared-expert work that spilled *out* of the gap in those
layers; the idle that remains is `vk-take` itself, 8.4 ms/token, and it sits
in the other layers (corrected at arbitration — the first draft of this
sentence called the 3.4 "the remaining idle").

There is no reordering left that recovers the 3.4: putting the shared expert after
the CPU share instead of before it is the same sum, and splitting it per layer
would need the gap length known in advance. The rest of that idle is Q8's
(a shorter GPU side) or Q7-gpu's (more work on the GPU), not Q3's.

### Serving A/B, `rome_bench.sh qwen38-vk`, interleaved, two repeats each

Same harness and eviction as every historical row, histogram frozen to a fresh
copy per run (`COLI_USAGE`), binaries selected with `ROME_BENCH_ENGINE_BIN`
(extended to the qwen targets in this commit so the four runs could be
interleaved without a rebuild in the middle of the A/B).

| run | rotating median | warm-identical | cold |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| q3-pristine-run1 | 4.05 | 5.27 | 3.52 |
| q3-candidate-run1 | 4.07 | 5.33 | 3.42 |
| q3-pristine-run2 | 4.04 | 5.26 | 3.47 |
| q3-candidate-run2 | 4.16 | 5.30 | 3.44 |

The pristine rows reproduce §Q-REBASE's 4.04/4.09 exactly, so the baseline is
the same one. Rotating **4.045 → 4.115 mean (+1.7%)**, both candidate runs above
both pristine runs, but the spread (+0.5% and +3.0%) is the harness's own —
this row is disk-dominated by design (`MEASURING.md`) and cannot resolve 2%.
**Warm-identical is the row that can**, because it pays no disk service:
5.27/5.26 → 5.33/5.30, **+0.9%**, which agrees with the fresh-process +1.5%
rather than contradicting it.

### What this changes for the roadmap

- Q3's own bucket is spent. `vk-take` is 8.4 ms/token, not 13.0.
- **Q5's re-scoping is now measured rather than argued.** §Q-PROFILE said
  making the hidden CPU side faster moves the counter and not the token; with
  8.4 ms/token of idle still in the gap, distributed so unevenly that a
  uniform 0.15 ms/layer of added CPU work was absorbed at 54%, a −2.1 ms
  `cpu-experts` win has even less room to become a token win than the roadmap
  assumed. Q5 should be expected to move `cpu-experts` and nothing else.
- The −18 to −28 ms/token quoted for Q3+Q1+Q2 becomes **−17 to −27** on Q3's
  measured share, and the "~163–173 ms/token after Q3+Q1+Q2+Q5" target moves to
  ~165–175 on this leg alone.

### Arbitration (Fable, 2026-09-12): accepted and merged, the gate corrected

Merged into `hot-expert-tier` (the merge commit carries the numbers above).
The two numeric legs were derived from the item's −5 to −7 estimate, and that
estimate assumed the shared expert would be absorbed in full — that every
layer's GPU wait exceeds that layer's shared-expert cost. The mean does
(0.263 vs 0.150 ms/layer); the distribution does not, and the item's own bound
"the smaller of 7.2 and 13.0" was a bound on the mean. The quantity a reorder
can claim is `Σ_l min(shared_l, wait_l)`, measured here at 3.98 ms/token (54%),
so a threshold of 5.0 sat above the mechanism's ceiling before the first run.
That is §G4's case — the ≤ 0.6 ms/call gate was unreachable through head
parallelism, the rotating median moved, and it merged with the reason recorded
— and it is decided the same way.

**What the gate should have read, and is now recorded as:** bit-identical;
`moe` down by at least half the shared bucket (≥ 3.6 ms/token) — below half, a
reorder is a prerequisite for Q8/Q7-gpu rather than a result on its own;
`Δvk-take` equal to `Δmoe` within the repeat spread, so the saving is idle
consumed and not work removed. Met at −4.14 / −4.21. The half is a judgment
floor set at arbitration, not a number derived from this run; it is written
down so the row stops reading as a miss to the next person.

Against rejecting: the unabsorbed 3.4 ms is not reachable by any reorder, and
the items that can act on it (Q8, Q7-gpu) change numerics, cost days, and need
the shared expert on this side of the issue to collect their own gains — holding
a bit-identical 2% behind them buys nothing. Against churn: Q7-gpu touches the
dense projections and Q8 the expert format and staging loop; neither re-orders
this function, and the diff is one hoisted scope in `c/qwen38_core.h`.

One correction to the section above, carried into the roadmap (Q3, Q5, §Q-ARB
paragraph): the first draft called the unabsorbed 3.4 ms "the remaining idle".
The idle that remains is `vk-take` itself, **8.41 ms/token** (12.62 − 3.98
absorbed; the 0.23 difference is `cpu-experts` noise); the 3.36 is
shared-expert work that spilled out of the gap in the layers whose wait was
already spent, and the two live in different layers. For sizing downstream: the
gap has 8.4 ms/token of idle, distributed so that a uniform 0.15 ms/layer of
added CPU work was absorbed at 54% — §Q-ARB's cold-expert eviction cost and
Q5's slack are to be priced at that rate, not at 100%; Q7-gpu spends the idle
in the slack layers and lengthens the GPU side in the CPU-long ones; Q8 shrinks
it. The 8.4 is not free in a layer that is already CPU-long.
